### 2.2 Assumptions (load-bearing)

**Reference-fibre block.**

- **A1 (reversible pair).** `A ∈ SL(2,ℤ)` hyperbolic with `tr A = 3`, `J` an involution with `JAJ = A⁻¹`, `tr J = 0`. There is `P ∈ GL(2,ℝ)` with `P⁻¹JP = σ_z`, `P⁻¹AP = e^{ησ_x}`, `cosh η = 3/2` (normal form; seed §6.1–6.4, elementary). Everything below is written in the normal form: `K := σ_x`, `J := σ_z`, `K' := iJK = −σ_y`, `A = e^{ηK}`. `η = arcosh(3/2) = 0.9624236501…`, `e^{η} = φ²`, `spec A = {φ², φ⁻²}`.
- **A2 (reference state).** The reference fibre is `ℂ²` with the Clifford triple `(K, K', J)`; a reference state is `σ = ½(I + m_K K + m_{K'} K' + m_J J)`, `|m| ≤ 1`. `m_J` is the **J-population** (timelike/spacelike population imbalance of the `g_L` splitting); `T² := m_K² + m_{K'}²` is the **odd coherence**. The coordinate triple `(K, K', J) = (σ_x, −σ_y, σ_z)` is left-handed relative to `(σ_x, σ_y, σ_z)`; cross products below are taken in the `(K, K', J)` coordinates.
- **A3 (return map on states — conditional reading).** One passive return acts on the reference state by the `h_L`-adjoint transfer `σ ↦ AσA / tr(AσA)` (in the normal form `h_L = I` and `A` is self-adjoint, so `A^♯ = A`). This is a filtering (non-unitary, trace-renormalised, **non-linear**) map, not a Hamiltonian evolution and **not a CPTP channel**: it is the success branch of an instrument. *This identification is the physical hypothesis of §9; §4.1–4.5 are exact algebra given A3.*
- **A3′ (canonical instrument completion — unconditional reading).** The filter of A3 is realised as the success branch `E₀ = A/‖A‖` of a two-outcome instrument `{E₀, E₁}`, `E₀²+E₁² = I`, with the canonical (positive-root) failure branch `E₁ = (I − E₀²)^{1/2}`. This is the Lüders operation of the two-effect observable `{F, I−F}`, `F = e^{−2η}A²` ([BS98] Eq. (1.3)); the unconditional return is the CPTP channel `Λ(σ) = E₀σE₀ + E₁σE₁`. The strength (`‖A‖ ↦ λ ≥ ‖A‖`) and failure-branch (`E₁ ↦ VE₁`) freedoms are declared in §10.1 and analysed in Remark 4.6.2. *A3′ is one canonical completion of A3 (minimal number of nonzero Kraus branches, maximal success probability for the prescribed filter), not a derivation of the physical instrument and not a uniqueness statement (NC-M68.8).*

**Carrier–reference coupling block.**

- **A4 (carrier).** The ZS-M67 carrier fibre `E₊ ≅ ℂ²` with grading `J_E = σ_z` and flip family `(X_E, Y_E) = (σ_x, σ_y)` (M67 Thm 19–20). The oriented soldering `ι₀` transports `(J, K, K')` onto `(J_E, X_E, −Y_E)` (seed §6.5; elementary).
- **A5 (collision model; fresh independent copies; subsystem/dilation hypothesis).** A record process is a repeated-interaction (collision) channel `Φ_σ(ρ) = Tr_R[U(ρ⊗σ)U†]`, `U = e^{−iαH}`, in which at step `n` a **fresh, independent** reference copy in the state `σ_n` is supplied, and `σ_n` evolves between steps by the passive return (A3 or A3′) **without back-action from earlier collisions**. *Scope note (v1.4; v1.3 audit §7).* The tensor factorisation `E₊ ⊗ R` with a partial trace over `R` is **itself a physical hypothesis**: ZS-M67's free-bulk discussion treats the boundary carrier as a pushforward of the bulk state, and an autonomous boundary-subsystem channel does not follow from the abstract fibre soldering `ι₀` alone. A5 therefore also asserts that a physical subsystem (or a dilation with a stated intertwiner and state restriction) exists for which the reduced CPTP map is the above collision channel. This is declared, not derived (`K17`, §10.2–10.3); it must be discharged by **step 0** of the S14 construction test of §6.7 (step 1 is the coupling tensor, which presupposes step 0). Its **record capacity** at a held reference `σ` is the `J_E`-component `κ_*(σ)` of the unique fixed point of `Φ_σ` (M67 Def. 40, Thm 25.1: a record requires a unique stationary state with `κ ≠ 0`); its **actual record** is the `J_E`-component `κ_n` of the carrier iterate `ρ_{n+1} = Φ_{σ_n}(ρ_n)`. The two are related by Lemma 5.10 and Prop 5.11, not identified.

**Boundary Dirac block (§8 only).**

- **B1.** 3+1 Dirac fermion on the half-space `x₃ ≥ 0`, tangential translation invariance, fixed tangential momentum `k_⊥ = |k|(cos q, sin q)`, `k_⊥ ≠ 0` unless stated.
- **B2.** Representation with `γ⁰, γ¹, γ³, γ₅` real and `γ²` imaginary (standard Dirac representation).
- **B3.** Chiral-bag condition `ψ(0) ∈ L_θ := ker(B_θ − 1)`, `B_θ = cos θ·(iγ³) + sin θ·(γ³γ₅)` (M67 Thm 3), any `θ`.
- **B4.** `H_k = γ⁰(γ¹k₁ + γ²k₂ − iγ³∂₃) + m(x₃)γ⁰e^{iθ_mγ₅} + μ(x₃)` with real **bounded** measurable `m, μ` and any real `θ_m`, on `D_θ = {ψ ∈ H¹(ℝ₊;ℂ⁴): ψ(0) ∈ L_θ}`.

### 2.3 Type firewall

| Object | Type | Prohibited identification |
|---|---|---|
| `2η = 2 arcosh(3/2)` | dimensionless rapidity increment per discrete return | a continuous-time rate; a clock increment (Mission §1.3: mapping required) |
| `ζ₀ = artanh m_K(0)` | reference rapidity along `K` | a time |
| Stokes 4-vector `(S₀, S_K, S_{K'}, S_J)` | Minkowskian under the transfer (filtering group of a `2×2` non-unitary map) | spacetime Lorentz symmetry (NC-M68.5) |
| `m_J` | J-population of the reference (resource of the soldered coupling) | odd coherence `T` (frame resource) |
| `m_w = m·w` | the reference component read by the coupling with resource axis `w = u×v` | "the record" by default (`m_J` is the case `w = e_J`) |
| `κ_*(σ)` | fixed-point `J_E`-component of the collision channel with **held** `σ`: a **record capacity** | the actual carrier record `κ_n` of the sequential process (related by Lemma 5.10); the lifetime of a physical record |
| `φ^{−4n}` (Thm 4.6) | the **reference-resource decay law** (transverse components of the reference under the unconditional return) | the lifetime of the carrier record (Prop 5.11: the carrier law also involves the collision relaxation `cos²2α`) |
| `m_K` (light-cone / longitudinal population) | conserved by the unconditional return (Thm 4.6), saturated by the conditional one (Thm 4.1) | a record resource *unless* `w_K ≠ 0` (Thm 5.8–5.9) |
| resource axis `w = u×v`; survival datum `w_K = w·e_K` | the direction whose reference component is the record resource; `w_K` decides survival in the conditional reading, and `w_K m_K(0)` in the unconditional one | the soldering orientation (fixes `w = e_J` only for the soldered coupling); the joint-grading parity (`w = ±e_J` is a proper subset of `w_K = 0`, Thm 5.8(b)); "the whole physical residue" (§6.7) |
| `T` (M60 odd coherence, `T² = m_K² + m_{K'}²`) vs `T_w` (transducer coherence, `T_w² = |m|² − m_w²`) | two different functionals of the same reference; they coincide **for every reference state iff** `w = ±e_J` (equivalently `ww^T = e_Je_J^T`; at one fixed state equality needs only `(m·w)² = m_J²` and can hold off that axis — e.g. the full-rank `m = (1/2,0,1/2)` with `w = e_K`, C70) | each other — an M60 floor on `T` is **not** a floor on `T_w`, so the K10 envelope of Cor 7.1 does not transfer to a general resource axis (W66; v1.3 audit F2) |
| `E₊ ⊗ R` and `Tr_R` (A5) | a declared subsystem/dilation on which the reduced collision channel is defined | a consequence of the soldering `ι₀` or of M67's boundary construction (K17) |
| `sup_α |κ_∞| = 2|w_K|/(1+w_K²)` (conditional reading, **`0 < |w_K| < 1`**) | a supremum over the collision angle, **not attained** in that range (`α = π/2` excluded); at the endpoints `|w_K| ∈ {0, 1}` it **is** attained at every admissible angle (values `0` and `1`; C68) | an attainable maximum in the interior; an "action-selected threshold"; a statement valid at the endpoints (v1.3 audit F4) |
| `𝒮_k` | antiunitary, position-preserving, `k_⊥`-dependent | a symmetry of the full 3+1 operator without fixing `k_⊥` |

### 2.4 Notation

`c_n := cosh 2nη`, `s_n := sinh 2nη`; `sech x := 1/cosh x`; `L_k, F_k` Lucas and Fibonacci numbers; `φ = (1+√5)/2`; `f := φ⁻⁴ = e^{−2η}`. For the collision channel, `r = (r_x, r_y, r_z)` is the carrier Bloch vector and `κ := r_z`; `c := cos 2α`. `D := 1 − (1−T²)c` (chiral coupling) and `D_w := 1 − (1−T_w²)c` (general resource axis). `P_⊥` is the projector on the transverse `(K', J)` plane of the reference Bloch vector. `x(ρ) := (1−√(1−ρ²))/ρ` is the inverse of `m ↦ 2m/(1+m²)` on `[0,1]`.

---

## 3. Reversible monodromy, oriented Clifford frame, soldering (brief; novelty NONE/low)

**Lemma 3.1 (normal form; elementary).** Under A1, `A = cosh η·I + sinh η·K` with `cosh η = 3/2`, `det A = 1`, `JAJ = A⁻¹`, `spec A = {φ², φ⁻²}` — in particular `|eig A| ≠ 1`, so `A` is not (conjugate to) a unitary and `σ ↦ AσA/tr` is a genuine filtering map. *(C01.)*

**Lemma 3.2 (oriented odd frame).** `K' := iJK` satisfies `K'^† = K'` (for the normal-form `h_L = I`; in general `K'^♯ = K'` with `X^♯ := h_L⁻¹X^†h_L`), `K'² = I`, `{J,K'} = {K,K'} = 0`, and its normal form is `−σ_y`. The soldering `ι₀` maps `K' ↦ iJ_EX_E = −Y_E`: the holonomy transports the **oriented** odd plane; the chirality is the residual `ℤ₂` sign of `K'` relative to the carrier's `Y_E`. *(C02; seed §6.5.)*

**Lemma 3.3 (reversal-blindness).** `Aⁿ = ½(L_{2n} I + √5 F_{2n} K)` and `Tr(f(A) J) = 0` for every polynomial `f`. *Proof.* `Aⁿ = e^{nηK} = cosh(nη) I + sinh(nη) K` and `e^{η} = φ²` give `cosh nη = L_{2n}/2`, `sinh nη = √5F_{2n}/2`; `Tr J = Tr KJ = 0`. The polynomial statement follows from the first display by linearity (Cayley–Hamilton reduces every polynomial in `A` to `aI + bK`); row C03 checks `n = 1..7` and is a witness for the polynomial claim, not its proof. ∎ Consequently no class function of the holonomy carries the **grading**: a `J`-population cannot be manufactured from `A`. *Scope (v1.4; v1.3 audit F1).* This is a statement about the `J`-component only. Functions of `A` do carry a **longitudinal** (`K`) population — indeed `K = (2A − 3I)/√5` and `A^q/tr(A^q) = ½(I + tanh(qη)K)` — and Corollary 5.12 shows that such a reference, read by a coupling with resource axis `w = e_K`, supports a faithful biased record. The lemma therefore must **not** be used to exclude an `A`-derived resource axis or an `A`-derived longitudinal reference (v1.3 §6.7 did so; withdrawn, D.5 F-1).

---

## 4. Return as a boost: filtering law, transverse depletion, `N`-return persistence, the canonical instrument

Throughout §4, A1–A3 hold and `σ = ½(I + m_K K + m_{K'} K' + m_J J)`.

### 4.1 Theorem 4.1 (full Bloch filtering law — rapidity addition)

**Theorem 4.1.** After `n` passive returns,
\[
m_K(n) = \frac{s_n + m_K c_n}{c_n + m_K s_n},\qquad
m_{K'}(n) = \frac{m_{K'}}{c_n + m_K s_n},\qquad
m_J(n) = \frac{m_J}{c_n + m_K s_n},
\]
and, writing `m_K(0) = tanh ζ₀` (`ζ₀ ∈ ℝ ∪ {±∞}`),
\[
\boxed{\;m_K(n) = \tanh(ζ_0 + 2nη),\qquad m_J(n) = m_J(0)\,\frac{\cosh ζ_0}{\cosh(ζ_0+2nη)},\qquad m_{K'}(n) = m_{K'}(0)\,\frac{\cosh ζ_0}{\cosh(ζ_0+2nη)}\;}
\]
Each return adds `2η` to the reference rapidity along `K`. Pure references stay pure and mixed references stay mixed.

*Proof.* Let `c = cosh nη`, `s = sinh nη`, `Aⁿ = cI + sK`, `M := 2σ = I + m_K K + m_{K'} K' + m_J J`. Using `K² = I` and `{K, K'} = {K, J} = 0`,
`KMK = I + m_K K − m_{K'} K' − m_J J` and `KM + MK = 2m_K I + 2K`. Hence
\[
AⁿMAⁿ = c²M + cs(KM+MK) + s²KMK = (c²+s²+2cs\,m_K) I + (2cs + (c²+s²)m_K) K + (c²−s²)(m_{K'}K' + m_J J),
\]
and `c² + s² = c_n`, `2cs = s_n`, `c² − s² = 1`. The trace is `2(c_n + m_K s_n)`, which is `≥ 2(c_n − s_n) = 2e^{−2nη} > 0` for `|m_K| ≤ 1`, so the normalisation is always defined; dividing gives the first display. For the second, put `m_K = tanh ζ₀`: `c_n + tanh ζ₀ s_n = cosh(ζ₀ + 2nη)/cosh ζ₀` and `(s_n + tanh ζ₀ c_n)/(c_n + tanh ζ₀ s_n) = tanh(ζ₀ + 2nη)`. Purity: `AⁿσAⁿ` has rank `rank σ` since `Aⁿ` is invertible, so pure states (rank one) stay pure; for mixed `σ`, `det(AⁿσAⁿ) = det σ > 0` (`det A = 1`) and division by the trace keeps the determinant positive. ∎ *(C04–C05, symbolic in `m` and `n`.)*

**Corollary 4.1.1 (the seed v1.1 law, correctly scoped).** For an initially J-polarised reference (`ζ₀ = 0`): `m_J(n) = 2m_J(0)/L_{4n}`, `m_K(n) = √5F_{4n}/L_{4n}`, since `cosh 2nη = L_{4n}/2`. In particular `m_J(1) = 2m_J(0)/7`.

### 4.2 Proposition 4.2 (return-invariant references)

**Proposition 4.2.** `AσA ∝ σ` if and only if `m = (±1, 0, 0)`: the two pure light-cone states (the `A`-eigenlines). Every return-invariant reference has `m_J = m_{K'} = 0`.

*Proof.* By Theorem 4.1 with `n = 1`, `m_J(1) = m_J/(c_1 + m_K s_1)` and likewise for `m_{K'}`. If `m_J ≠ 0` or `m_{K'} ≠ 0`, invariance forces `c_1 + m_K s_1 = 1`, i.e. `m_K = −(cosh 2η − 1)/sinh 2η = −tanh η`; but then `m_K(1) = m_K` reads `sinh 2η + m_K cosh 2η = m_K`, i.e. `m_K = −coth η`, and `|coth η| > 1`, contradiction. Hence `m_J = m_{K'} = 0`, and `m_K(1) = m_K` gives `sinh 2η(1 − m_K²) = 0`, so `m_K = ±1`. Conversely `A(I ± K)A = e^{±2η}(I ± K)`. ∎ *(C06, exact solve.)*

*Meaning (RQ2).* This is the fibre-level form of `return ≠ repetition`: the only states for which base-point return is full-state return are the two null states, and they carry no transverse population. **A nonzero transverse population implies non-return-invariance**; the converse is false (`m = (0, m_{K'}, 0)` is non-invariant, and so is `m = (0, 0, 0)` with `m_J = 0` — no transverse population is implied by non-invariance). The null states carry a full longitudinal population `m_K = ±1`, which is exactly the resource of the tilted couplings of §5.8–5.9.

### 4.3 Theorem 4.3 (the return is a Lorentz boost of the reference Stokes vector)

**Theorem 4.3.** On the un-normalised Stokes 4-vector `S = (tr σ, tr σK, tr σK', tr σJ)` one transfer `σ ↦ AⁿσAⁿ` acts as
\[
Λ_n = \begin{pmatrix} c_n & s_n \\ s_n & c_n\end{pmatrix} \oplus \mathbf 1_2,\qquad Λ_n^{\mathsf T}\,\mathrm{diag}(1,-1,-1,-1)\,Λ_n = \mathrm{diag}(1,-1,-1,-1),
\]
a pure boost of rapidity `2nη` along `K`; the components `S_{K'}, S_J` are transverse and untouched, and the normalised Bloch components are depleted by the Lorentz factor `1/(c_n + m_K s_n)`.

*Proof.* Read off the un-normalised coefficients in the proof of Theorem 4.1; `Λ_n` preserves the Minkowski form because `c_n² − s_n² = 1`. ∎ *(C07.)*

**Imported-result firewall.** *Imported theorem:* a non-unitary `2×2` (Jones) filter acts on the Stokes 4-vector as a Lorentz transformation; attenuation filters are squeezes/boosts (Han–Kim–Noz, PRE 56, 6065 (1997), full text read; §12). *Status:* IMPORTED-PROVEN. *What is new here:* the specialisation to the squeeze `A = e^{ηK}` of the trace-three reversible class, whose rapidity `2η = 2 arcosh(3/2)` is a conjugacy invariant of that class; the identification of the transverse component `S_J` with the `g_L`-timelike/spacelike splitting; the light-cone fixed points as `A`-eigenlines. *Status of mapping:* DERIVED (A1–A3). The Lorentz group here is the filtering group of the map, **not** spacetime symmetry (NC-M68.5).

### 4.4 Theorem 4.4 (transverse depletion: exact law and universal bound)

**Theorem 4.4.** For every reference state with finite `ζ₀`, both transverse components obey the exact law `m_J(n) = m_J(0)cosh ζ₀ · sech(ζ₀ + 2nη)`, `m_{K'}(n) = m_{K'}(0)cosh ζ₀ · sech(ζ₀ + 2nη)`, and therefore
\[
\boxed{\;|m_J(n)|,\ |m_{K'}(n)| \le \operatorname{sech}(ζ_0 + 2nη) \xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{} 0,\qquad \operatorname{sech}(ζ_0+2nη) \le 2e^{-(ζ_0+2nη)} = 2e^{-ζ_0}\,φ^{-4n}\;}
\]
with equality in the first bound for `m_J` exactly on the pure states with `m_{K'} = 0` (and for `m_{K'}` on the pure states with `m_J = 0`). The prefactor `m_J(0)cosh ζ₀ ∈ [−1, 1]` is state-dependent: references with `m_J(0) = 0` stay at zero, and only the pure meridian states saturate the bound.

*Proof.* The exact law is Theorem 4.1. The Bloch constraint gives `m_J(0)² ≤ 1 − m_K(0)² − m_{K'}(0)² ≤ 1 − tanh²ζ₀ = sech²ζ₀`, i.e. `|m_J(0)| cosh ζ₀ ≤ 1`, with equality iff `m_{K'}(0) = 0` and the state is pure. The second bound is `sech x ≤ 2e^{−x}` for `x ≥ 0` and `e^{2η} = φ⁴`. ∎ *(C08.)*

*Scope.* `ζ₀ = ±∞` are the null states of Proposition 4.2 (transverse components already zero). The longitudinal component is *not* depleted: `m_K → ±1`. **The holonomy sharpens the frame (`T → 1`) while erasing the transverse populations (`m_J, m_{K'} → 0`)** — whether that is "erasing the record" depends on which reference component the coupling reads (§5.8–5.9). *(v1.0/v1.1 wrote "decay as `sech(ζ₀+2nη)`"; the audit's F9 point that this conflates the bound with the state-dependent law is accepted; the front matter now states the law and the bound separately.)*

### 4.5 Theorem 4.5 (`N`-return persistence ceiling; sharp; target-blind)

**Theorem 4.5.** Let `N ≥ 2`. If `|m_J(n)| ≥ r > 0` at `N` consecutive returns `n = n₀, …, n₀+N−1`, then
\[
\boxed{\; r \le \operatorname{sech}\big((N-1)η\big)\;}\qquad\text{so } r \le \tfrac23 \ (N=2),\quad r \le \tfrac27 \ (N=3),
\]
and, for the trace-`T` reversible class, `sech η = 2/T`. The bound is attained: the pure reference with `ζ₀ + 2n₀η = −(N−1)η`, `m_{K'} = 0` has `|m_J(n)| = sech(ζ₀ + 2nη)` along its whole orbit, hence `min_{n} |m_J(n)| = sech((N−1)η)` over the window.

*Proof.* By Theorem 4.4, `sech(x_n) ≥ r` at `x_n := ζ₀ + 2nη`, i.e. `|x_n| ≤ arcosh(1/r)`, for `N` consecutive values of `n`; these are spaced by `2η`, so `2(N−1)η = x_{n₀+N−1} − x_{n₀} ≤ 2 arcosh(1/r)`, i.e. `cosh((N−1)η) ≤ 1/r`. For the pure orbit, `m_J(0) = sech ζ₀` gives `m_J(n) = sech ζ₀·cosh ζ₀/cosh(x_n) = sech x_n` exactly (Theorem 4.1), and the symmetric window `x ∈ {−(N−1)η, …, (N−1)η}` has minimum `sech((N−1)η)`. `sech η = 1/cosh η = 2/3`; `sech 2η = 1/(2cosh²η − 1) = 2/7`. ∎ *(C09: the constants `N = 2, 3` and the pure-orbit identity; the general-`N` statement is the written argument.)*

*Remark 4.5.1.* Theorem 4.5 is a statement about the **population** `m_J` (or `m_{K'}`, verbatim). Its translation into a statement about the fixed-point **capacity** `κ_*` needs the amplification bound of Theorem 5.3; that translation is Corollary 5.4.

### 4.6 Theorem 4.6 (canonical instrument completion of the return; the reference-resource decay law: the unconditional return is K-dephasing at `φ⁻⁴`)

The filter of A3 is not a channel. One canonical physical completion (A3′) is:

**Theorem 4.6.** Let `E₀ := A/‖A‖ = e^{−η}A` and `E₁ := (I − E₀²)^{1/2}`. Then
(i) `E₁ = √(1 − φ⁻⁸) P₋`, `P₋ = ½(I − K)`: the failure branch projects the reference onto the **negative null state**, which carries no transverse population (`m_J = m_{K'} = 0`, Prop 4.2);
(ii) the success probability is `p(σ) = tr(E₀σE₀) = e^{−2η}(cosh 2η + m_K sinh 2η) = e^{−2η} cosh(ζ+2η)/cosh ζ`, with `p → 1` on the positive null state and `p = e^{−2η}` at `m_K = −tanh η`;
(iii) the unconditional channel `Λ(σ) = E₀σE₀ + E₁σE₁` is **exact dephasing in the `K` (light-cone) basis**:
\[
\boxed{\;m_K \mapsto m_K,\qquad (m_{K'}, m_J) \mapsto φ^{-4}\,(m_{K'}, m_J),\qquad φ^{-4} = e^{-2η} = \tfrac{7-3\sqrt5}{2} = 0.1459…\;}
\]
Hence, unconditionally, after `n` returns `m_J(n) = φ^{−4n} m_J(0)` **exactly and linearly, for every reference** (no dependence on `ζ₀`), and the fixed points of `Λ` are precisely the `K`-diagonal states.

*Proof.* In the `K`-eigenbasis `A = diag(e^{η}, e^{−η})`, so `E₀ = diag(1, e^{−2η})` and `I − E₀² = diag(0, 1 − e^{−4η})`, giving (i). (ii) is `tr(A²σ)e^{−2η}` with `A² = cosh 2η I + sinh 2η K`. (iii) Both Kraus operators are diagonal in the `K`-basis, so `Λ` preserves the `K`-diagonal entries (`m_K` invariant) and multiplies the off-diagonal entry by `1·e^{−2η} + 0 = e^{−2η}`; `e^{2η} = φ⁴`. The fixed-point statement is the commutant statement for a commutative two-effect Lüders operation ([LW10], main theorem; here elementary). ∎ *(C30, symbolic in `m`.)*

**What this theorem is (type; v1.2).** Theorem 4.6 is the **reference-resource decay law**: it describes the reference, not the carrier. The carrier record's evolution under a sequence of collisions with such references is Proposition 5.11, and its lifetime is *not* `φ^{−4n}` in general. v1.1's statement that "the unconditional linear law supplies the lifetime of a changing record" (v1.1 §0.2 A-8, §2.3, §9.2, §11.3) is withdrawn (audit F4).

**Imported-result firewall (v1.2).** *Imported:* the two-branch positive-square-root completion `ρ ↦ F^{1/2}ρF^{1/2} + (I−F)^{1/2}ρ(I−F)^{1/2}` of an effect `F` is the Lüders operation of an unsharp two-valued observable ([BS98], Eq. (1.3)); for a two-effect (hence commutative) family its Heisenberg-picture fixed points are the commutant of `F` ([BS98] Prop. 2; [LW10]). *Status:* IMPORTED-PROVEN. *What is new here:* the effect `F = e^{−2η}A²` of the trace-three class, for which the failure branch is rank one onto the negative null state and the dephasing factor is the class invariant `φ⁻⁴`; and the reading of the K-diagonal fixed points as the conserved light-cone population. *Status of mapping:* DERIVED (A1, A3′). The dephasing *form* is therefore not a new instrument principle (audit §9); the specialisation is.

*Remark 4.6.1 (what changes between the readings).* Conditionally (A3) the reference is driven to the positive null state, `m_K → 1`, and the transverse components decay as `cosh ζ₀/cosh(ζ₀+2nη)`; unconditionally (A3′) `m_K` is **conserved** and the transverse components decay as `φ^{−4n}`. In both readings the transverse components die and the longitudinal one does not. The conditional decay can be slower than `φ^{−4n}` at early returns (a negative `ζ₀` lets `m_J` grow before it falls, Thm 4.5) but is paid for by the success probability (Thm 4.7).

*Remark 4.6.2 (strength and failure-branch freedoms; §10.1; corrected in v1.2, audit F5).*
(a) *Strength.* Any instrument whose success branch is `A/λ` with `λ ≥ ‖A‖` and canonical failure branch `E₁ = (I − A²/λ²)^{1/2}` is again K-dephasing, with factor
`f(λ) = λ⁻² + √((1−e^{2η}/λ²)(1−e^{−2η}/λ²))`, `f(‖A‖) = φ⁻⁴`, `f ↑ 1` as `λ → ∞`. **`f` is strictly increasing in `λ`** (exactly, not on samples): with `t = λ⁻² ∈ (0, φ⁻⁴]` and `a+b := e^{2η}+e^{−2η} = 7`, `f = t + √(1−(a+b)t+t²)`, and `df/dt < 0` because `(a+b) − 2t > 0` on that range and `((a+b)−2t)² − 4(1−(a+b)t+t²) = (a+b)² − 4 = 45 > 0`. The canonical `λ = ‖A‖` is the **strongest** unconditional depletion; Theorems 6.5–6.6 use only `|f| < 1`. *(C32, exact.)*
(b) *Failure branch.* Write `Q := I − A²/λ² = (1 − cosh2η/λ²)I − (sinh2η/λ²)K` and replace `E₁` by `V Q^{1/2}` with `V` unitary. Then the completed channel `Λ_V(σ) = E₀σE₀ + VQ^{1/2}σQ^{1/2}V†` is **unital iff `VQV† = Q` iff `VKV† = K`** (for `η > 0`, finite `λ`), i.e. iff `V` is diagonal in the `K` basis. **`V ≠ I` does not imply a non-unital completion**: `V = e^{iπK/4}` is a non-trivial unitary that leaves `Λ_V` unital, and at maximal strength (`Q = (1−φ⁻⁸)P₋`, rank one) any such `V` changes only a Kraus phase — the whole channel is unchanged. A `V` that rotates the `K` axis (e.g. `e^{iπJ/4}`) gives a non-unital completion. The description "the failure branch re-prepares the reference in the pure state `V|−⟩`" is correct **only at maximal strength** `λ = ‖A‖` (rank-one `Q`); for `λ > ‖A‖`, `Q` is full rank (e.g. `det Q = 19/81` at `λ = 3`) and the failed state is input-dependent. Theorem 6.5 applies to every K-diagonal completion (canonical `V = I` or any `V` commuting with `K`) whose transverse multiplier has modulus `< 1`; a K-rotating `V` is the K15 case. *(C51.)* v1.1 wrote "`V ≠ I` is a non-unital feedback (re-preparation in `V|−⟩`)", which is false as stated; corrected here.

### 4.7 Theorem 4.7 (success-probability × transverse-reference-retention identity)

**Theorem 4.7.** Along `n` post-selected returns, with `p_k` the success probability at return `k`,
\[
\boxed{\;\Big(\prod_{k=1}^{n} p_k\Big)\cdot\frac{m_J(n)}{m_J(0)} \;=\; e^{-2nη} = φ^{-4n}\qquad\text{for every reference with } m_J(0) \ne 0,\;}
\]
and `∏ p_k = e^{−2nη} cosh(ζ₀+2nη)/cosh ζ₀ → (1 + m_K(0))/2`: the cumulative survival of post-selection converges to the weight of the reference on the positive null state. The same identity holds for `m_{K'}`.

*Proof.* `∏ p_k = tr(AⁿσAⁿ)e^{−2nη} = e^{−2nη}(c_n + m_K s_n)` and `m_J(n)/m_J(0) = 1/(c_n + m_K s_n)` (Theorem 4.1); the un-normalised `S_J = tr(AⁿσAⁿJ) = m_J(0)` is return-invariant (Theorem 4.3, transverse component). The limit uses `c_n + m_K s_n → e^{2nη}(1+m_K)/2`. ∎ *(C31.)*

*Reading (corrected in v1.2, audit F4).* This is an identity about the **reference population**, not about a carrier record. The un-normalised invariance `tr(AⁿσAⁿJ) = m_J(0)` is an algebraic identity of the filter, **not an instrument-level conservation law**: in the canonical instrument the failure branch stores no transverse expectation at all — `Tr(J E₁σE₁) = Tr(K' E₁σE₁) = 0` for every `σ` (C50) — so the transverse signal is not "diluted into the failure branch" (v1.1 wording, withdrawn). What is measurable is the success-weighted transverse signal, which decays as `f^n m_J(0)`.

---

## 5. The record transducer

Throughout §5, A4–A5 hold. `Φ_σ` is the collision channel with a held reference state `σ`; `r ↦ Mr + b` is its affine action on the carrier Bloch vector.

### 5.1 Theorem 5.1 (a commutative coupling cannot record)

**Theorem 5.1.** Let the carrier–environment interaction be `H = Σ_i f_i(X_E) ⊗ B_i` with `f_i` polynomials and `B_i` arbitrary environment operators (in particular `H = X_E ⊗ B`), let the environment be in any state and `α` arbitrary. Then every Kraus operator of `Φ` lies in the commutative algebra `ℂ[X_E] = span{I, X_E}`, and consequently `Φ` is **unital** and **flip-covariant**: `Φ(X_EρX_E) = X_EΦ(ρ)X_E`. By ZS-M67 Theorem 25.1 (upstream), any unique stationary state of such a channel has `κ = 0`: no record.

*Proof.* `U = e^{−iαH}` is a power series in `H`, each term of which lies in `ℂ[X_E] ⊗ 𝔅(ℋ_env)`; with `ρ_env = Σ_k p_k|ψ_k⟩⟨ψ_k|` and any orthonormal basis `{e_j}` of the environment, the Kraus operators `E_{jk} = √p_k⟨e_j|U|ψ_k⟩` are partial matrix elements and therefore lie in `ℂ[X_E]`. A commutative Kraus family satisfies `Σ E E^† = Σ E^† E = I` (the second is trace preservation), so `Φ` is unital. For the unitary `X_E ∈ ℂ[X_E]`, `Φ(X_EρX_E) = Σ E X_E ρ X_E E^† = X_E(Σ EρE^†)X_E`. ∎ *(C12: symbolic, three-level environment, generic pure environment state, generic `α`; the arbitrary-dimension statement is the written argument.)*

*No-Go Emission Gate record (kernel §5.5).* SIGN: the gate asked "can a coupling through one odd direction record?"; the computed object is flip-covariance, which M67 Thm 25.1 converts to `κ = 0` — the negative answer is what is computed, not a tautology. QUANTIFIER: ∀ environment, ∀ state, ∀ `α` (proof, not sample). SOURCE: structural (commutativity of the coupling algebra), not a failure of my construction. VALUE: fixes the complement as **non-commutative carrier coupling algebras** — a necessary condition. It does **not** make two odd *reference* directions necessary (audit A-4): `X_E⊗K + Y_E⊗J` records the odd coherence `m_{K'}` with the same law (Theorem 5.8, `w = u×v`). PASSED (with the complement stated correctly).

### 5.2 Theorem 5.2 (chiral record-transfer law)

**Theorem 5.2.** Let `H_χ = X_E ⊗ K + χ Y_E ⊗ K'`, `χ ∈ {+1, −1}` (chirality), `U = e^{−iαH_χ}`, and `σ = ½(I + m_K K + m_{K'} K' + m_J J)`. Then:

(i) `Φ_σ(r) = Mr + b` with
\[
b = -χ\, m_J \sin^2 2α\; e_J,\qquad
M = \begin{pmatrix} \cos 2α & 0 & χ\,m_{K'}\sin 2α\\ 0 & \cos 2α & -m_K \sin 2α \\ -\tfrac{\chi\, m_{K'}\sin 4α}{2} & \tfrac{m_K \sin 4α}{2} & \cos^2 2α\end{pmatrix}.
\]
For `sin 2α ≠ 0`: `b = 0` iff `m_J = 0`; the channel is non-unital iff `m_J ≠ 0`, and its flip-covariance defect is nonzero iff `m_J ≠ 0` or `m_{K'} ≠ 0`. (At `sin 2α = 0`, `b = 0` for every reference, but the channel is the identity only for `α ∈ πℤ`; for `α ∈ π/2 + πℤ`, `U = −χ Z⊗Z` — the global phase is the **chirality** — and the channel is `Ad_{J_E}` on the carrier for both signs, `M = diag(−1,−1,1)` — C63; v1.2 wrote "the identity" for all excluded angles (audit v1.2 F5), and v1.3 printed the phase as `−Z⊗Z` for both chiralities (audit v1.3 F5; C63 now checks `U = −χ Z⊗Z` per chirality). The quantifier `sin 2α ≠ 0` was missing in v1.0/v1.1; audit F3/L-3.)

(ii) `det(I − M) = 2 sin²2α sin²α (1 − (1−T²)cos 2α)`, so the fixed point is unique iff `sin 2α ≠ 0`, and then
\[
\boxed{\;κ_* = -χ\, m_J\,\frac{1-\cos 2α}{1-(1-T^2)\cos 2α},\qquad r_x^* = -\,\frac{m_J m_{K'}\sin 2α}{D},\qquad r_y^* = χ\,\frac{m_J m_K \sin 2α}{D},\qquad D := 1-(1-T^2)\cos 2α .\;}
\]
`κ_* = 0` iff `m_J = 0`; `sign κ_* = −χ·sign m_J` (the sign of the record is the chirality); for a diagonal reference (`T = 0`), `κ_* = −χ m_J` independently of `α`.

*Proof.* (i) In the normal form `H_χ = σ_x⊗σ_x − χ σ_y⊗σ_y`; `H_χ² = 2(I + χ σ_z⊗σ_z)` (v1.0/v1.1 printed a minus sign here, which reverses the active parity block; corrected, C55) has eigenvalues `0, 4`, so `U = e^{−iαH}` is computed exactly in closed form (it is a rotation by `2α` in the two-dimensional eigenspace of `H²` at `4`). Compute `Tr_R[U(ρ⊗σ)U†]` symbolically for `ρ = ½(I + r·σ)` and read off the affine map; this is row C13 (symbolic in `m, α, χ`). (ii) The determinant and the solution of `(I − M)r = b` are exact symbolic computations (C14). Positivity of `D`: for `c ∈ (−1,1)` and `T² ∈ [0,1]`, `D = 1 − (1−T²)c ≥ 1 − |c| > 0` (a uniform argument; the endpoint values are `D = 1 − c` at `T = 0` and `D = 1` at `T = 1`, which v1.0/v1.1 had interchanged; C55). The `T = 0` case is `κ_* = −χ m_J (1−cos2α)/(1−cos2α)`. ∎ *(C13–C14; V17 reproduces the fixed point by numerical iteration with an independent numeric engine; W18 is a 20 000-sample faithfulness witness, superseded by the exact identity of Theorem 5.7.)*

**Imported-result firewall (scope corrected in v1.2, v1.0 audit §7).** *Imported:* for thermal (diagonal) ancillas and `XX`/`YY` couplings in the **short-collision** regime, the steady population equals the ancilla population when `J_xx = J_yy` and is inverted when `J_xx`, `J_yy` have opposite signs ([CM25], Eqs. (15)–(16)); this magnitude-independence is a short-collision statement — at finite collision time ([CM25] Eq. (14)) it fails in general (the v1.0 auditor exhibited `ω_S = ω_A = 1`, `J_xx = 2`, `J_yy = −1`, `τ = π/√10` with `p_S^* = p_A`). The equal-magnitude opposite-sign coupling of this paper is not affected by that counterexample. The `T = 0` fixed point is homogenisation ([SZ02]). *Status:* IMPORTED-PROVEN (in the stated scope). *What is new here:* the typing of the resource (`m_J`, the `g_L` J-population, not `T`), and the exact affine law at finite collision angle for an **arbitrary coherent** reference, in which population and coherence do **not** decouple and the denominator `D` depends on `T²`. *Status of mapping:* DERIVED (A4–A5); novelty of the coherent extension: `OPEN-NOVELTY` (that coherence of the ancilla changes the steady state is known in general, e.g. [RSS19]; the exact finite-angle fixed point and bound were not found).

### 5.3 Theorem 5.3 (record amplification bound; replaces the seed inequality) — v1.2 form

**Theorem 5.3.** With `g(c, T²) := (1−c)/(1−(1−T²)c)`, `c = cos 2α ∈ (−1, 1)`:
(i) `∂g/∂c = −T²/D² ≤ 0`, so `g` is non-increasing in `c`; `g(0, ·) ≡ 1`; `g(c, 0) ≡ 1`;
(ii) `sup_{c∈(−1,1)} g = 2/(2 − T²)`, and exactly
\[
\frac{2}{2-T^2} - g(c,T^2) \;=\; \frac{T^2(1+c)}{(2-T^2)\,D} \;\ge\; 0,\qquad\text{with equality iff } T = 0;
\]
so for `T > 0` the supremum is approached as `α → π/2` and **not attained**, while for `T = 0` it is attained at every `c` (`g ≡ 1`); (v1.1 stated "not attained" without the `T > 0` qualifier; audit L-1.)
(iii) for every reference state, using `T² ≤ 1 − m_J²`,
\[
\boxed{\;|κ_*| \;\le\; \frac{2|m_J|}{2-T^2}\;\le\;\frac{2|m_J|}{1+m_J^2}\;\le\;1,\;}
\]
where the first inequality is **strict iff `m_J ≠ 0` and `T > 0`** (equality at `T = 0`, where `κ_* = −χ m_J`, and trivially at `m_J = 0`); the second is an equality iff **`m_J = 0` or `T² = 1 − m_J²` (pure reference)** — exactly, `2m/(1+m²) − 2m/(2−T²) = 2m(1−m²−T²)/((2−T²)(1+m²))` with `m = |m_J|` (v1.1 said "pure reference only"; audit L-2; C55); and the third is an equality iff `|m_J| = 1`. Moreover `|κ_*| − |m_J| = −|m_J|T²c/D`, so `|κ_*| = |m_J|` iff `m_J T² c = 0` (`T = 0` or `cos 2α = 0` or `m_J = 0`); for `m_J ≠ 0`, `T > 0`: `|κ_*| ≤ |m_J|` iff `cos 2α ≥ 0`, and a coherent reference **amplifies** the record iff `cos 2α < 0`.

*Proof.* (i) direct differentiation; (ii) the displayed identity is algebra (C15), and `1 + c > 0`, `D > 0`; (iii) `2/(2−T²)` is increasing in `T²` and `T² ≤ 1 − m_J²`; the second-gap identity is algebra (C55); `2m/(1+m²) = 1 − (1−m)²/(1+m²) ≤ 1`. ∎ *(C15 symbolic; W15b boundary instances `(T=0, m_J=3/5)` — equality — and `m_J = 0`; W16 counter-witness to the seed: `(4/5, 0, 3/5)`, `α = 3π/8`, `|κ_*| = (615 + 240√2)/1169 = 0.8164 > 0.6`.)*

### 5.4 Corollary 5.4 (record persistence under passive return — the comparison, made after freezing)

*Reading.* Let the reference at return `n` be `σ_n = A^nσ_0A^n/tr`, and define the **record capacity** of `σ_n` as the fixed-point record `κ_*(σ_n)` of the collision channel with `σ_n` held fixed (A5). This is the reading under which "the reference carries a record of magnitude `ρ`" is a well-typed statement; the actual sequential record `κ_n` is a different object (Lemma 5.10 says it converges to the limiting capacity; Prop 5.11 says its transient is not the capacity), and the v1.0 auditor exhibited a strongly polarised carrier that keeps a high polarisation through more than three weak collisions — Corollary 5.4 is a capacity statement, **not a universal lifetime bound**.

**Corollary 5.4.** (i) `|κ_*(σ_n)| ≤ 2|m_J(n)|/(1+m_J(n)²) ≤ 2 sech(ζ₀+2nη)/(1 + sech²(ζ₀+2nη)) → 0`: **for the soldered chiral coupling, passive holonomy destroys the record capacity of every reference** (conditional reading); unconditionally, `|κ_*(Λⁿσ)| ≤ 2φ^{−4n}|m_J(0)|/(1 + φ^{−8n}m_J(0)²) → 0` (Theorem 4.6).
(ii) `|κ_*| ≥ ρ` requires `|m_J| ≥ x(ρ) := (1 − √(1−ρ²))/ρ` (inverse of `m ↦ 2m/(1+m²)` on `[0,1]`). Hence a record capacity `≥ ρ` at `N` consecutive passive returns requires `sech((N−1)η) ≥ x(ρ)`.
(iii) *Comparison (locked input `T₂ = 0.835381287313630`, entered only here, in §5.9(iv) and rows C19/V57):* `x(T₂) = 0.53907…`, and `sech 2η = 2/7 = 0.2857 < 0.5391 < 2/3 = sech η`. Exactly: `x(ρ) ∈ (2/7, 2/3) ⇔ 28/53 < ρ < 12/13`, and the decimal `T₂` read as a rational satisfies both strict inequalities (C19, exact). **Therefore a record capacity of magnitude `T₂` can be held by a passively returning reference through at most two consecutive returns and never three.** Two returns are not excluded by this bound: the pure reference at the two-return ceiling (`|m_J| = 2/3`, `T² = 5/9`) with `α → π/2` reaches `|κ_*| → 12/13 = 0.923 > T₂` (a supremum, by Theorem 5.3(ii)).

(iv) *Finite-angle witness (v1.3; derived by the v1.2 auditor).* The pure references `m^{(0)} = (−√5/3, 0, 2/3)` (rapidity `−η`) and `m^{(1)} = (+√5/3, 0, 2/3)` — exactly one passive return apart (Theorem 4.1: `tanh η = √5/3`) — both have `T² = 5/9` and, at the admissible angle `cos 2α = −3/5` (`D = 19/15`), held-reference capacity `|κ_*| = 16/19 = 0.84210… > T₂` with faithfulness margin `T⁴/D² = 625/3249 > 0`. **A `T₂`-magnitude capacity at two consecutive passive returns is therefore attained at a finite admissible angle**, not only approached as a supremum. This is a held-reference capacity witness; it does not say that the actual carrier reaches `16/19` within two collisions (Lemma 5.10 gives the limit, Prop 5.11 the transient), and it is not evidence of action selection. *(C62, exact.)*

*Proof.* (i) Theorems 5.3 and 4.4. (ii) `2m/(1+m²) ≥ ρ ⇔ m ≥ x(ρ)` for `m ∈ [0,1]`. (iii) Theorem 4.5 with `r = x(T₂)`; `2m/(1+m²)` at `m = 2/7, 2/3` equals `28/53, 12/13`; exact rational comparison C19. (iv) `κ_* = m_J g(c, T²)` with `g(−3/5, 5/9) = (8/5)/(19/15) = 24/19`, `(2/3)(24/19) = 16/19`; the orbit step is Theorem 4.1 at `n = 1`. ∎

*What was withdrawn.* The seed's "cannot be carried through even one return" used `|κ_*| ≤ |m_J|`. The corrected statement is weaker for `N ≤ 2` and exact for `N ≥ 3`. The **structural conclusion for the soldered coupling is unchanged**: a persistent record requires an operation in the cycle that competes with the holonomy (§6) — non-unital on the reference (Theorem 6.5) — *or* a coupling whose resource axis has `w_K ≠ 0` (Theorem 5.9).

### 5.5 Proposition 5.5 (two Clifford modes, not two species)

**Proposition 5.5.** The two resources of the corpus two-species model — odd reference transfer (`H`-mode) and population drift (`B`-mode) — are the two Clifford modes of **one** rank-two reference: a non-commutative carrier coupling algebra (Theorem 5.1, necessary) and a reference component `m_w` along the coupling's resource axis (Theorem 5.2/5.8, `b ∝ m_w`). One carrier with a Clifford-pair coupling realises both; the two-species model is the case in which the modes live on different tensor factors. *(Consequence of Theorems 5.1–5.2, 5.8; the corpus-branch text was directly read in seed v1.3.)*

### 5.6 Necessary condition and sufficient construction, separated

- *Necessary (Theorem 5.1):* the carrier-side coupling algebra must be non-commutative; otherwise the channel is unital and flip-covariant and `κ = 0`.
- *Sufficient (Theorem 5.2):* the chiral Clifford-pair coupling with a reference having `m_J ≠ 0`.
- *Neither necessary nor characteristic:* "two odd reference directions". The coupling `X_E⊗K + Y_E⊗J` (one odd, one even reference direction) records `m_{K'}` with the same law (Theorem 5.8; it is the frame `(u,v,w) = (e_K, e_J, −e_{K'})`). What is characteristic is the **resource axis** `w` of Theorem 5.8, and what decides survival under passive return is its light-cone projection `w_K` (Theorem 5.9).

### 5.7 Theorem 5.7 (exact faithfulness identity; replaces witness W18)

**Theorem 5.7.** For the fixed point of Theorem 5.2(ii),
\[
\boxed{\;1 - |r^*|^2 \;=\; \frac{T^4 \;+\; \big(1-|m|^2\big)\big(T^2\sin^2 2α + (1-\cos 2α)^2\big)}{D^2},\qquad |m|^2 = T^2 + m_J^2 .\;}
\]
Both numerator terms are `≥ 0`. Hence the fixed point is **faithful (mixed, `|r^*| < 1`) iff not (`T = 0` and `|m_J| = 1`)**, i.e. for every reference except the two pure diagonal ones, for which `r^* = (0, 0, ∓1)` (homogenisation to a pure carrier state, [SZ02]); and the faithfulness margin obeys `1 − |r^*|² ≥ T⁴/D² ≥ T⁴/4`.

*Proof.* `|r^*|² = m_J²[T² sin²2α + (1−c)²]/D²` from Theorem 5.2(ii). The algebraic identity `D² − (1−T²)[T²(1−c²) + (1−c)²] = T⁴` (expand `D = 1 − (1−T²)c`) gives `D² − m_J²[…] = T⁴ + (1 − T² − m_J²)[…]`. ∎ *(C28 symbolic; W29 the non-faithful instance.)*

### 5.8 Theorem 5.8 (coupling-class covariance; symmetry classification and resource-axis classes) — v1.2 form

**Theorem 5.8.** Let `R ∈ SO(3)` act on the reference Bloch coordinates `(m_K, m_{K'}, m_J)`, let `(u, v, w) := (R e_K, R e_{K'}, R e_J)` (so `w = u × v` in these coordinates), and let
\[
H_R := X_E ⊗ (u·\vec σ_R) + χ\, Y_E ⊗ (v·\vec σ_R),\qquad \vec σ_R := (K, K', J).
\]
(a) *Covariance.* `Φ^{H_R}_σ = Φ^{H_χ}_{V†σV}` for the `SU(2)` element `V` implementing `R`, so the affine map, the fixed point and Theorems 5.2, 5.3, 5.7 hold verbatim with
\[
m_J \;\to\; m_w := m·w,\qquad T^2 \;\to\; T_w^2 := |m|^2 - m_w^2 ,
\]
in particular `κ_* = −χ m_w (1−cos 2α)/(1−(1−T_w²)cos 2α)`, `|κ_*| ≤ 2|m_w|/(1+m_w²)`: **the record resource of `H_R` is the component of the reference along `w = u×v`.**
(b) *Symmetry classification.* The joint grading acts as `Ad_{J_E⊗J}(X_E⊗(u·σ_R)) = X_E ⊗ ((u_K, u_{K'}, −u_J)·σ_R)` (and likewise on the `Y_E` term). Hence
\[
[H_R,\ J_E⊗J] = 0 \iff u_J = v_J = 0 \iff w = \pm e_J .
\]
(c) *Resource-axis classes.* Two couplings with the same `w` have the same resource `m_w`, the same `T_w`, hence the same record `κ_*` for every reference; they need not have the same Hamiltonian or the same carrier fixed point: rotating `(u, v)` by `θ` inside the plane `w^⊥` keeps `w` (and, for `w = ±e_J`, the joint-grading symmetry) but changes `H_R` and the affine matrix `M` for a fixed coherent reference (W46: `θ = 0.7` changes `H` by Frobenius norm `1.9397` and `M` by `0.2472`). The soldered chiral coupling (`R = 1`, `w = e_J`) is therefore the unique **resource-axis class** (up to `w ↦ −w`, i.e. `χ ↦ −χ`) whose resource is the grading population, and the unique class that is joint-grading symmetric — **not** a unique Hamiltonian (v1.1 wrote "unique member"; audit F1).
(d) *Geometry.* `e_K ∈ span(u, v) ⇔ w_K = 0` (since `w ⊥ span(u,v)`): the coupling plane **contains** the light-cone axis exactly when the resource axis has **no** light-cone component. (v1.1 §0, its §6.6 and the abstract had this reversed: "coupling plane contains `K` ⇒ record survives"; corrected. The surviving record is read by the *normal* component `w_K`.)

*Proof.* (a) `H_R = (I⊗V)H_χ(I⊗V)†`, so `U_R = (I⊗V)U(I⊗V)†` and `Tr_R[U_R(ρ⊗σ)U_R†] = Tr_R[U(ρ⊗V†σV)U†]` (invariance of the partial trace under a unitary on the traced factor). `V†σV` has Bloch vector `R⁻¹m`, whose `J`-component is `m·(Re_J) = m·w`. Any `R ∈ SO(3)` in the `(K, K', J)` coordinates is implemented by some `V ∈ SU(2)` (the triple differs from `(σ_x, σ_y, σ_z)` by the orientation flip `K' = −σ_y`, which conjugates `R` by `diag(1,−1,1)` and preserves `SO(3)`; the cross product is taken in the `(K,K',J)` coordinates throughout). (b) `J_E X_E J_E = −X_E`, `J_E Y_E J_E = −Y_E`, and `J(u·σ_R)J = (−u_K, −u_{K'}, u_J)·σ_R`; the product gives the stated sign `(u_K, u_{K'}, −u_J)` (v1.1's proof printed `(−u_K, −u_{K'}, u_J)`, the sign of the factor `J` alone; the conclusion `u_J = v_J = 0` is unchanged, C44). Since `X_E⊗·` and `Y_E⊗·` are linearly independent, `[H_R, J_E⊗J] = 0` iff `u_J = v_J = 0` iff `w = ±e_J`. (c) `κ_*` depends on `σ` only through `(m_w, T_w)` by (a); the in-plane rotation is `R ↦ R·R_z(θ)`, which fixes `Re_J = w`; the numerical instance is W46. (d) linear algebra. ∎ *(C36: symbolic for the one-parameter tilt `u = cos β e_K + sin β e_J`, `v = e_{K'}`, `w = (−sin β, 0, cos β)`, all `m`, `α`; V38 independent numerics; C44 symbolic for (b); the general-`R` covariance is the written argument, D43; the v1.1 auditor's direct `4×4` computation of 200 generic `SO(3)` frames of both chiralities agreed with (a) to `7.7×10⁻¹⁵`, evidence bundle.)*

*Prior-art typing.* Reference-frame covariance of a collision channel is elementary (novelty NONE); what is used is only which reference component the holonomy conserves.

### 5.9 Theorem 5.9 (survival classification of the limiting record by the light-cone projection `w_K`) — v1.2 form

**Theorem 5.9.** Let the reference undergo passive returns, let the coupling be `H_R` with resource axis `w`, `w_K := w·e_K`, and let `sin 2α ≠ 0`. All statements concern the **limiting held-reference capacity** `κ_∞ := κ_*(σ_∞)` at the limit reference `σ_∞`; that the actual sequential process reaches it is Lemma 5.10.
(i) *Conditional reading (A3), `m_K(0) > −1`.* `m(n) → e_K` exponentially (`|m(n) − e_K| = O(φ^{−4n})`, Theorem 4.1) and
\[
\boxed{\;κ_∞ = -χ\,w_K\,\frac{1-\cos 2α}{1-w_K^2\cos 2α},\qquad 1-|r^*_∞|^2 = \frac{(1-w_K^2)^2}{(1-w_K^2\cos 2α)^2}.\;}
\]
The limiting record is **biased iff `w_K ≠ 0`**, **faithful iff `|w_K| < 1`**, hence **biased and faithful iff `0 < |w_K| < 1`**. At `|w_K| = 1` (`w = ±e_K`) the limiting carrier state is the pure pole `r^*_∞ = (0,0,−χ w_K)` (W47). *Exception:* `m_K(0) = −1` (the negative null state) is return-invariant, `σ_∞ = σ_0`, and `κ_∞ = +χ w_K(1−cos2α)/(1−w_K²cos2α)`.
(ii) *Unconditional reading (A3′).* `m(n) → (m_K(0), 0, 0)` (Theorem 4.6) and
\[
κ_∞ = -χ\,w_K\,m_K(0)\,\frac{1-\cos 2α}{1-\big(1-m_K(0)^2(1-w_K^2)\big)\cos 2α}\qquad (T_w^2 = m_K(0)^2(1-w_K^2)),
\]
**biased iff `w_K m_K(0) ≠ 0`**, **faithful iff `|w_K m_K(0)| < 1`** (Theorem 5.7 with `|m| = |m_K(0)|`), hence **biased and faithful iff `0 < |w_K m_K(0)| < 1`**. In particular a reference with `m_K(0) = 0` (e.g. the maximally mixed reference, or any initially K-unpolarised reference) yields **no** unconditional bias for any resource axis (W47); and **`|w_K| = 1` with `0 < |m_K(0)| < 1` yields a biased and faithful unconditional record without refresh** (`κ_∞ = −χ w_K m_K(0)`, i.e. `−χ m_K(0)` at `w = +e_K` and `+χ m_K(0)` at `w = −e_K`; `1 − |r^*_∞|² = 1 − m_K(0)²`; direct witnesses at `m_K(0) = 1/2`, `χ = +1`: `r^* = (0,0,−1/2)` for `w = +e_K`, `r^* = (0,0,+1/2)` for `w = −e_K`, margin `3/4` in both; the reference is return-invariant): the pure pole of (i) is a fact of the *conditional* reading only (C60; v1.2 audit F1). *(v1.3 printed `κ_∞ = −χ m_K(0)` without the factor `w_K` and its row tested only the positive axis; corrected here — audit v1.3 F3.)*
(iii) *Erased class.* For every coupling with `w_K = 0` both limits vanish. This class is `{w ⊥ e_K}`, the **transverse-resource class**; it **contains the joint-grading-even class `w = ±e_J` as a proper subset** (Theorem 5.8(b)). *Converse, per reading:* conditionally (`m_K(0) > −1`) `κ_∞ = 0` **iff** `w_K = 0`; unconditionally `κ_∞ = 0` **iff** `w_K m_K(0) = 0` (the numerator of (ii) is `−χ w_K m_K(0)(1−c)` and `D_w ≥ 1 − |c| > 0`), so the unconditional erased set is `{w_K m_K(0) = 0}`, which contains `{w_K = 0}` together with every initially K-unpolarised reference (C60). v1.2's summaries wrote "vanishes iff `w_K = 0`" for both readings; withdrawn (audit v1.2 F1). Minimal witness (W45): the proper frame `(u, v, w) = (e_K, −e_J, e_{K'})`, `H = X_E⊗K − χY_E⊗J`, has a joint-grading-odd component (Frobenius defect `‖[J_E⊗J, ·]`-type‖ = 4) and `w_K = 0`, and its conditional limiting record is `κ_∞ = 0`. The v1.1 headline "every coupling with a joint-grading-odd component carries a persistent record" is **false** and withdrawn; "`w_K = 0` (joint-grading-even)" as an identification is withdrawn (audit F1).
(iv) *Attainability of a target in the conditional model (v1.2; audit F3; scope made explicit in v1.3).* Put `a := |w_K| ∈ (0,1)`, `c = cos 2α ∈ (−1,1)`, `k(a,c) := a(1−c)/(1−a²c) = |κ_∞|` in (i) — the conditional limiting record; the unconditional optimum depends on `m_K(0)` as well, (v). Then
\[
\sup_{c\in(-1,1)} k(a,c) = \frac{2a}{1+a^2},\qquad \frac{2a}{1+a^2} - k(a,c) = \frac{a(1-a^2)(1+c)}{(1+a^2)(1-a^2c)} > 0 ,
\]
so for `0 < a < 1` the supremum is **not attained** at any admissible angle. *At the endpoints it is attained:* the gap factor `a(1−a²)` vanishes iff `a ∈ {0,1}`, and there `k(a,c) ≡ 0` resp. `≡ 1` at every admissible angle, equal to `2a/(1+a²)` (C68; the unqualified "not attained" of the v1.3 abstract and type firewall is corrected — audit v1.3 F4; in the conditional reading `a = 1` is separately excluded by faithfulness, which is a different reason). For a target `ρ ∈ (0,1)`:
- `sup_α |κ_∞| ≥ ρ` iff `a ≥ x(ρ)` (supremal/closure formulation);
- **there exists an admissible `α` with `|κ_∞| ≥ ρ` iff `a > x(ρ)`** (strict), and the admissible angles are exactly `c ≤ (a−ρ)/(a(1−ρa))`, `−1 < c < 1`;
- if faithfulness is also required, `x(ρ) < a < 1`.
At the excluded angle `α = π/2` the chiral channel is `M = diag(−1,−1,1)`, `b = 0` for every reference (C49, C63): every `J_E`-diagonal carrier state is fixed and no unique record exists. *Comparison, made after freezing (locked `T₂`; rows C19/V57/W64 only):* a `T₂`-magnitude record is attained faithfully at some admissible angle in the conditional reading **iff `x(T₂) < |w_K| < 1`, exactly**, where `x(T₂) = (1−√(1−T₂²))/T₂ = 0.5390701239…`. The decimal is an approximation, not the boundary: `|w_K| = 0.53908 < 0.5391` with `cos 2α = −0.99999` already gives `|κ_∞| = 0.835387… > T₂` (W64; v1.2 wrote "iff `0.5391 < |w_K| < 1`", withdrawn — audit v1.2 F6). For the tilt parametrisation `w = (−sin β, 0, cos β)`, `β ∈ (−π/2, π/2]`: `|sin β| > x(T₂)`, i.e. `|β| > 32.62°` (approximately). At `|w_K| = x(T₂)` exactly, every admissible angle gives `|κ_∞| < T₂` (e.g. `α = 1.56`: `0.83533 < 0.83538`, V57). v1.1's "reachable iff `|w_K| ≥ 0.539`" is withdrawn.
(v) *Unconditional supremum (audit F6).* In (ii), with `b₀ := m_K(0)`, `sup_α |κ_∞| = 2|w_K b₀|/(2 − b₀²(1−w_K²))` (C54) — a function of `(w_K, b₀)`, not of `w_K` alone.

*Proof.* (i) Insert `m = e_K` (`m_w = w_K`, `T_w² = 1 − w_K²`, `|m| = 1`) into Theorems 5.8(a), 5.7; the exception is Prop 4.2 with `m = −e_K`. (ii) Insert `m = m_K(0)e_K`: `m_w = w_K m_K(0)`, `T_w² = m_K(0)²(1−w_K²)`. (iii) `w_K = 0` gives `m_w → 0` in both readings; the witness is a direct `4×4` computation (W45). (iv) The gap identity is algebra (C48); `2a/(1+a²)` is increasing on `[0,1]` with inverse `x`; the admissible-angle condition is `k(a,c) ≥ ρ` solved for `c` (C48: `k(a, c_ρ) = ρ` at `c_ρ = (a−ρ)/(a(1−ρa))`, and `c_ρ = −1` iff `ρ = 2a/(1+a²)`). (v) Theorem 5.3(ii)–(iii) with `m_w = w_K b₀`, `T_w² = b₀²(1−w_K²)`. ∎ *(C37 symbolic — the v1.1 draft's denominator error was caught by this row before release; V38 numerics on the positive null state; W45, W47, C48, C49, C54, V57.)*

*Reading (formal, not physical).* The passive holonomy does not destroy "the record"; it destroys the **transverse** reference resource and conserves or saturates the **longitudinal** one. Whether the carrier reads a transverse or a longitudinal component is decided by the light-cone projection `w_K` of the coupling's resource axis — the survival datum this paper cannot supply (§6.7, NC-M68.7). `w_K` fixes the limiting record's value and purity only together with `(α, χ)` and, unconditionally, `m_K(0)`; it does not fix the frame, the instrument or the reference supply (NC-M68.10).

### 5.10 Lemma 5.10 (actual sequential fresh-copy collisions reach the limiting record) — v1.2; derived by the v1.1 auditor

**Lemma 5.10.** Fix `H_R`, `χ` and `α` with `sin 2α ≠ 0`. Under A5 let `σ_n` be the reference supplied at step `n` (fresh, independent copies; `σ_n` follows the passive return of A3 or A3′), let `r_{n+1} = M_n r_n + b_n` be the carrier iterate with `(M_n, b_n)` the affine data of `Φ_{σ_n}`, and suppose `σ_n → σ_∞` (which holds in the conditional reading for `m_K(0) > −1`, exponentially, and in the unconditional reading for every reference, exponentially). Then:
(a) for **every** reference `σ`, the characteristic polynomial of the affine matrix `M_σ` is
\[
(λ - c)\,\big[λ^2 - c(1+c)\,λ + d\big],\qquad d := c\big[c^2 + q(1-c^2)\big],\qquad q := T_w^2 \in [0,1],
\]
and all its roots lie in the open unit disc: `M_σ` is Schur-stable, uniformly in `σ` for fixed `c`;
(b) `r_n → r_∞`, the unique fixed point of `Φ_{σ_∞}`, and the convergence is exponential.

*Proof.* (a) By Theorem 5.8(a) it suffices to take `w = e_J` with `(m_K, m_{K'}, m_J)` read in the rotated frame, `T_w² = q`. Expanding `det(λI − M)` from Theorem 5.2(i) gives the stated factorisation (the off-diagonal products contribute `−T² sin²2α cos 2α`, so only `q` enters; C52 symbolic). The linear factor has `|c| < 1`. For the quadratic `λ² + a₁λ + a₀`, `a₁ = −c(1+c)`, `a₀ = d`, the Jury conditions are `|a₀| < 1`, `1 + a₁ + a₀ > 0`, `1 − a₁ + a₀ > 0`: first, `d = c[1 − (1−q)(1−c²)]` gives `|d| ≤ |c| < 1`; second, `1 − c(1+c) + d = (1−c²)[1 − (1−q)c] > 0` since `(1−q)c < 1`; third, `p(q) := 1 + c(1+c) + d` is affine in `q` with slope `c(1−c²)`: for `c ≥ 0` every term of `p` is non-negative and `p ≥ 1`; for `c < 0` the slope is negative so `p(q) ≥ p(1) = (1+c)² > 0`. (C52 checks the three identities symbolically.) (b) Choose a vector norm `‖·‖_*` with `‖M_∞‖_* ≤ a < 1` (possible for a Schur-stable matrix). Since `σ ↦ (M_σ, b_σ)` is polynomial in `m`, `M_n → M_∞`, `b_n → b_∞`, and for `n ≥ n₀`, `‖M_n‖_* ≤ a' < 1`. With `e_n := r_n − r_∞` and `r_∞ = M_∞r_∞ + b_∞`,
\[
e_{n+1} = M_n e_n + (M_n - M_∞) r_∞ + (b_n - b_∞),
\]
so `‖e_{n+1}‖_* ≤ a'‖e_n‖_* + ε_n` with `ε_n → 0` — exponentially if `σ_n → σ_∞` exponentially — hence `e_n → 0` (exponentially, with rate `max(a', rate of ε_n) + o(1)`). ∎ *(C52; V53: direct `4×4` sequential iteration in both readings, error `< 10⁻¹¹` after 140 collisions; the v1.1 auditor's independent implementation checked the characteristic polynomial on 200 generic frames.)*

*Scope.* The lemma upgrades the limiting **capacity** of Theorem 5.9 to the limit of the **actual** carrier record — within A5's fresh-independent-copy model only. **Schur stability is not one-step strict contractivity:** at `m = e_K`, `α = π/4` the affine part is `M = [[0,0,0],[0,0,−1],[0,0,0]]` (nilpotent, `ρ(M) = 0`) but `‖M‖₂ = 1`, and the carrier states `(I±Z)/2` are mapped to `(I∓Y)/2`, preserving trace distance `1` in one collision (W59). Contraction holds in an adapted norm (as used in (b)) and for `M²`, not for `Φ_σ` itself in the Euclidean/trace distance. v1.2 §10.3-7 claimed strict contractivity of `Φ_σ` "through Schur stability"; withdrawn (audit v1.2 F2). It does not supply a physical fresh-reference source, does not include back-action on a re-used reference, and does not make `φ^{−4n}` the carrier lifetime (Prop 5.11). Uniqueness of `r_∞` uses `det(I − M_∞) ≠ 0`, i.e. `sin 2α ≠ 0` (Theorem 5.2(ii)).

### 5.11 Proposition 5.11 (carrier record dynamics under the unconditional return; the lifetime is not `φ^{−4n}`) — v1.2; derived by the v1.1 auditor

**Proposition 5.11.** Take the soldered coupling (`w = e_J`), the unconditional return (A3′), fresh copies `σ_n = Λⁿσ_0` with `σ_0 = ½(I + m₀J)` (K-unpolarised, so `m(n) = (0, 0, m₀fⁿ)` and `M_n = diag(c, c, c²)`, `b_n = −χ(1−c²)m₀fⁿ e_J`). Then the carrier record obeys exactly
\[
κ_{n+1} = c^2 κ_n - χ(1-c^2)\, m_0 f^n ,\qquad
κ_n = (c^2)^n κ_0 - χ(1-c^2)m_0\frac{(c^2)^n - f^n}{c^2 - f}\ (c^2 \ne f),\qquad κ_n = f^n κ_0 - χ(1-f)m_0\, n f^{n-1}\ (c^2 = f).
\]
Hence the carrier record's decay is governed by **both** the collision relaxation `c² = cos²2α` and the reference law `f = φ⁻⁴`; in general `κ_{n+1} ≠ f κ_n`. Exact instance (direct `4×4`, C50): `α = π/6`, `χ = +1`, `m₀ = 1/2`, `ρ₀ = I/2` give `κ₁ = −3/8`, `κ₂ = (−45 + 18√5)/32`, `κ₂ − fκ₁ = −3/32 ≠ 0`. For a general reference the third row of `M` couples `κ` to `(r_x, r_y)` and the scalar recurrence does not close; the general statement is Lemma 5.10 (Schur-stable relaxation with eigenvalues depending on `α` and `σ_n`).

*Proof.* Theorem 5.2(i) at `m = (0,0,m_J)` and Theorem 4.6; the closed forms are the solution of the linear recurrence (C52-type algebra, checked in C50). ∎

*Consequence (type correction; audit F4).* "Capacity" and "lifetime" remain **distinct obligations**: Theorem 4.6 gives the reference law, Lemma 5.10 the convergence of the actual record to the limiting capacity, and this proposition the transient. v1.1 §0.2 A-8 / §2.3 / §9.2 / §11.3, which declared the capacity/lifetime objection closed by the linear law, are withdrawn.


### 5.12 Corollary 5.12 (holonomy-derived longitudinal references are read faithfully; the reversal-blindness lemma does not exclude them) — v1.4; statement from the v1.3 audit, proof here

*Motivation.* v1.3 §6.7 listed among the "must not" items that the survival datum "be a function of `A` alone (Lemma 3.3)". Lemma 3.3 proves `Tr(f(A)J) = 0` — the absence of a **`J`-population** in any function of the holonomy. It says nothing about a **longitudinal** population, and `K` itself is a function of `A`. The following corollary makes the surviving branch explicit; the v1.3 prohibition is withdrawn (Appendix D.5, F-1).

**Corollary 5.12.** Under A1, A3′, A4–A5 and `sin 2α ≠ 0`, put, for `q > 0`,
\[
K \;=\; \frac{2A - 3I}{\sqrt5},\qquad
\sigma_q \;:=\; \frac{A^q}{\operatorname{tr} A^q} \;=\; \tfrac12\big(I + \tanh(q\eta)\,K\big) ,
\]
both functions of `A` alone (for integer `q ≥ 1`, `σ_q` is a normalised polynomial in `A` with two positive eigenvalues), and take the proper frame `(u, v, w) = (e_{K'}, e_J, e_K)`, i.e. `H_R = X_E⊗K' + χ Y_E⊗J`, so that `w_K = 1`. Then:
(i) `Tr(σ_q J) = Tr(σ_q K') = 0` (consistent with Lemma 3.3) while `m_K = tanh(qη) ∈ (0,1)`;
(ii) `T_w = 0`, the held-reference fixed point is unique for every admissible angle, and
\[
\boxed{\;κ_*(\sigma_q) \;=\; -χ\,\tanh(q\eta),\qquad r^*_x = r^*_y = 0,\qquad 1-|r^*|^2 \;=\; \operatorname{sech}^2(q\eta) \;>\; 0\;}
\]
independently of `α` — a **biased and faithful** record;
(iii) `σ_q` is invariant under the unconditional return `Λ` of A3′ (every `K`-diagonal state is), so this is the **unconditional limiting** record (Theorem 5.9(ii) at `|w_K| = 1`, `m_K(0) = tanh(qη)`), reached by the actual sequential process (Lemma 5.10); under the conditional return `σ_q ↦ σ_{q+2}`, so the conditional limit is the positive null state and the conditional limiting record is the pure pole of Theorem 5.9(i);
(iv) *instance.* `q = 2`, `χ = +1`, `α = π/4`: `σ_2 = ½(I + (3√5/7)K)`, `M = 0`, `b = (0,0,−3√5/7)`, so **every** carrier state reaches the fixed point in one collision, with faithfulness margin `4/49`.
*Comparison, made after the family is fixed (locked `T₂`, `M_*`; row W66):* `tanh 2η = 3√5/7 = 0.95831… > T₂` and the odd coherence of `σ_2` is `T = 3√5/7 > M_*`, while `tanh η = √5/3 = 0.7454 < T₂`.

*Proof.* (i) `A^q = e^{qηK} = cosh(qη)I + sinh(qη)K`, `tr A^q = 2cosh(qη)`, and `Tr(KJ) = Tr(KK') = 0`; `K = (2A−3I)/√5` follows from `A = (3/2)I + (√5/2)K` (Lemma 3.1). (ii) By Theorem 5.8(a) the record depends on `σ` only through `m_w = m·w = tanh(qη)` and `T_w² = |m|² − m_w² = 0`; Theorem 5.2(ii) with `T_w = 0` gives `κ_* = −χ m_w(1−c)/(1−c) = −χ m_w` for every `c ∈ (−1,1)`, and `r^*_x = r^*_y = 0` because `m_{K'} = m_J = 0` in the rotated frame; Theorem 5.7 gives `1 − |r^*|² = 1 − m_w² = sech²(qη)`. Uniqueness is `det(I−M) = 2sin²2α sin²α(1−c) ≠ 0`. (iii) `Λ` fixes `m_K` and multiplies the transverse components by `φ⁻⁴` (Theorem 4.6), and `σ_q` has none; the conditional statement is Theorem 4.1 (`ζ ↦ ζ + 2η`, i.e. `q ↦ q + 2`). (iv) direct `4×4` computation at `c = 0`. ∎ *(C67: symbolic in the longitudinal population, in `α` and in both chiralities; the `q = 2` instance exact.)*

**Scope — what this is and is not.** The state and the resource direction come from `A`, but the *frame* uses `J` as well (`H_R = X_E⊗K' + χY_E⊗J`), and nothing here derives `q`, the frame, `α`, `χ`, the instrument, the reference supply or the readout from the Z-Spin action (NC-M68.1, NC-M68.11). The corollary refutes one prohibition and shows the survival branch of Theorem 5.9(ii) is non-empty even when the reference is manufactured by the holonomy itself; it does not make that branch physical, and the capacity comparison in the last line is made after the family is fixed (K11). In the original non-orthogonal integral coordinates the construction must be transported with the `h_L` inner product and the normal form (Lemma 3.1).

---

## 6. Holonomy creates the frame but erases the transverse resource: the refresh requirement (K12a/b/c) and the survival datum (K12d)

### 6.1 Definition 6.1 (the map-level question; K12a)

Let `ℋ` be one passive return on the reference state (A3) and `ℛ` any preparation/refresh operation the action supplies between physical records. The well-typed question is whether the action derives the **complete one-cycle map** `𝒞 = ℛ∘ℋ` (or the action-derived order) together with a physical invariant or periodic state `σ_*` with
\[
𝒞(σ_*) = σ_*,\qquad |m_w(σ_*)| > 0,
\]
and — only after freezing — the required record capacity. No scalar threshold on `ℛ` is universal without specifying its generator class; `2η` is the rapidity increment of `ℋ`, not a property of `ℛ`. A continuous-time rate condition is admissible only after an action-derived return duration/clock exists (**K12c**; Mission §1.3 forbids identifying `arg λ`, holonomy phase and physical time increment without a mapping).

### 6.2 Theorem 6.2 (constant same-axis counterboost subclass; K12b)

**Theorem 6.2.** Restrict `ℛ` to a constant same-axis filter `B_χ = e^{−(χ/2)K}` (acting as `σ ↦ B_χσB_χ/tr`), which subtracts rapidity `χ` per cycle. Then `ζ_{n+1} = ζ_n + 2η − χ`, so `ζ_n = ζ₀ + n(2η − χ)` and, since `|m_J(n)| ≤ sech ζ_n`, an initially nonzero **transverse** resource `m_J` (the resource of the soldered coupling; the same for `m_{K'}`) persists asymptotically **iff `χ = 2η` exactly**: `χ < 2η` under-corrects and `χ > 2η` overshoots (`ζ_n → −∞`), both erasing the record. Moreover at `χ = 2η` the one-cycle map is the **identity**, `B_{2η}A = I`.

*Proof.* Same-axis filters compose by rapidity addition (Theorem 4.1 applied to `e^{−(χ/2)K}`, which is `e^{ηK}` with `η ↦ −χ/2`); the drift coefficient of `n` vanishes iff `χ = 2η`; `B_{2η} = cosh η I − sinh η K = A⁻¹`. ∎ *(C10; the seed v1.3 K12 suite rows K12-C2/C3/W4 were re-run in the v1.0 session 8/8 — that run is **not invoked** by the v1.2 verifier and its artifact is not in this package; D43.)*

*Scope (v1.3; audit v1.2 F7).* The "iff" concerns the persistence of a transverse resource that is nonzero at the start; it is not a necessary condition for the light-cone record of a tilted coupling (`w_K ≠ 0`), which needs no counterboost at all (Theorem 5.9), nor for references with `m_J(0) = 0`.

*Consequence.* Within the constant same-axis subclass the only persistent cycle is the trivial one that undoes the return exactly. **Any non-trivial persistent cycle must lie outside this subclass**: outside the whole unital class in the unconditional reading (Theorems 6.5–6.6), or outside the transverse-resource coupling class (Theorem 5.9).

### 6.3 Proposition 6.3 (arbitrary CPTP re-preparation defeats any universal threshold)

**Proposition 6.3.** For `|r| ≤ 1`, `ℛ_r(X) := Tr(X)σ_r`, `σ_r = ½(I + rJ)`, has Choi matrix `I ⊗ σ_r ≥ 0` (eigenvalues `(1±r)/2`) and is trace preserving; after every return it restores `m_J = r` independently of the incoming rapidity. The target-blind witness `r = 3/4 > 2/3` exceeds the two-return ceiling with no rapidity parameter at all.

*Proof.* Direct. ∎ *(C11.)* This is **not** a physical solution: `σ_r` and `ℛ_r` are inserted by hand and fail the action-selection requirement (Mission RQ3; M67 Def. 40 S1). It proves only that the missing object is an **action-selected map/state**.

### 6.4 Theorem 6.4 (the involutive holonomy cycle `𝒞 = Ad_J∘ℋ`; the v1.0 audit's construction)

**Theorem 6.4.** Let `ℋ(σ) = AσA/tr` (A3) and `𝒞 := Ad_J∘ℋ`, `Ad_J(σ) = JσJ`. Then:
(i) `𝒞² = id` on all states (indeed `JA(JAσAJ)AJ = σ` un-normalised, by `JAJ = A⁻¹`, equivalently `(JA)² = I`): the cycle "return, then reverse the loop orientation" is an involution;
(ii) the `𝒞`-fixed states are exactly `m = (−tanh η, 0, m_J) = (−√5/3, 0, m_J)` with `|m_J| ≤ sech η = 2/3` — the reference family at rapidity `−η`, on which two consecutive returns hold the same `|m_J|`; **only its pure endpoints `|m_J| = 2/3` are the `N = 2` extremal orbit of Theorem 4.5** (the mixed members hold equal two-return populations strictly below the ceiling; v1.1 called the whole family "the extremal orbit" — corrected, audit §7.2);
(iii) for **every** reference `m_J` has period 2 under `𝒞`: `m_J(2k) = m_J(0)`, `m_J(2k+1) = m_J(0) cosh ζ₀/cosh(ζ₀+2η)`; the record capacity `κ_*` (a function of `m_J` and `T²` only) is likewise period 2 and is invariant under `Ad_J` itself;
(iv) the success probability of the filter on the fixed family is `p = e^{−2η} = φ⁻⁴` per cycle, so the fixed family survives `n` cycles with probability `φ^{−4n}` (Theorem 4.7 with retention 1); for an arbitrary reference the two-cycle Kraus product is `(JA)(JA) = I` up to the normalisation `e^{−2η}` per success, so survival through `2k` successful cycles has probability `f^{2k}`.

*Proof.* (i) algebra. (ii) `Ad_J` flips `(m_K, m_{K'})` and fixes `m_J`; by Theorem 4.1 with `n = 1`, `𝒞(σ) ∝ σ` iff `m_K(1) = −m_K`, `m_{K'}(1) = −m_{K'}`, `m_J(1) = m_J`; the first gives `s₁(1+m_K²) + 2m_K c₁ = 0`, i.e. `m_K ∈ {−tanh η, −coth η}`, of which only `−tanh η` is admissible; then `c₁ + m_K s₁ = 1`, so `m_J(1) = m_J` automatically and `m_{K'}(1) = m_{K'} = −m_{K'}` forces `m_{K'} = 0`; the Bloch ball gives `|m_J| ≤ √(1−tanh²η) = sech η`. (iii) `ℋ` adds `2η` to `ζ`, `Ad_J` maps `ζ ↦ −ζ`; two cycles return `ζ` to itself and `m_J` scales by `cosh ζ₀/cosh(ζ₀+2η) · cosh(ζ₀+2η)/cosh ζ₀ = 1`. (iv) Theorem 4.6(ii) at `m_K = −tanh η`; `(JA)² = I`. ∎ *(C33.)*

*Reading.* This is a **post-selected** refresh: it uses only holonomy data `(A, J)` and keeps every record capacity periodic, but it does not select a state (a one-parameter family survives) and it pays `φ⁻⁴` in probability per cycle. Unconditionally it fails — Theorems 6.5–6.6.

### 6.5 Theorem 6.5 (no unital refresh has a transverse fixed point unconditionally)

**Theorem 6.5.** Let `Λ` be the unconditional return channel of Theorem 4.6, or any K-diagonal completion of Remark 4.6.2 (canonical failure branch or `V` commuting with `K`, strength `λ ≥ ‖A‖`) whose transverse multiplier `f` has `|f| < 1`, and let `ℛ` be any **unital** CPTP map on the reference (every unitary, every mixture of unitaries, `Ad_J` in particular). Then every fixed point of `ℛ∘Λ` and of `Λ∘ℛ` has `m_J = m_{K'} = 0`. Consequently, in the unconditional reading, **no unital operation in the cycle can sustain a record for any coupling with `w_K = 0`** (the transverse-resource class, the soldered coupling included), while the longitudinal component `m_K` is unconstrained.

*Proof.* A unital CPTP map is a contraction of the Bloch ball in the Euclidean norm: `|ℛ(r)| ≤ |r|`. (Two routes: (1) unital trace-preserving positive maps are contractive in every Schatten `p`-norm with `p > 1` ([PWPR06], `p = 2`), and `‖σ‖₂² = (1 + |r|²)/2`; (2) Kadison–Schwarz, `ℛ(X)†ℛ(X) ≤ ℛ(X†X)`, traced. IMPORTED.) By Theorem 4.6 / Remark 4.6.2, `|Λ(r)|² = m_K² + |f|²(m_{K'}² + m_J²) ≤ |r|²` with equality iff `m_{K'} = m_J = 0`. If `r = ℛ(Λ(r))` then `|r| ≤ |Λ(r)| ≤ |r|`, so `|Λ(r)| = |r|` and the transverse part vanishes. If `r = Λ(ℛ(r))` then `|r| = |Λ(ℛ r)| ≤ |ℛ r| ≤ |r|`, so `ℛ r` has no transverse part and `r = Λ(ℛ r) = ℛ r` has none either. ∎ *(C34 executable half; W35 retyped in v1.2: rotations about `e_K` have the fixed vector `e_K` (transverse part zero, found); for a rotation `R`, `R·diag(1,f,f)` has a fixed vector iff `R e_K = e_K` — so the 3 000 generic rotations of v1.1 found none, which was vacuous as a transverse test and is now reported as consistent with this lemma; the spectrum `{−1, −φ⁻⁴, φ⁻⁴}` of `Ad_J∘Λ` is checked exactly.)*

**Imported-result firewall (v1.2).** *Imported:* for `p > 1`, a positive trace-preserving map is Schatten-`p` contractive iff it is unital ([PWPR06]). *What is new here:* nothing about contractivity; the theorem is the direct consequence "strict transverse contraction of the dephaser + non-expansion of the refresh ⇒ no transverse fixed point". Novelty typing changed from `OPEN-NOVELTY` (v1.1) to **IMPORTED principle / direct consequence** (audit §9).

**No-Go Emission Gate (kernel §5.5) — PASSED for this class.** SIGN: the question "can a unital refresh keep the transverse record unconditionally?" is answered by computing the fixed points — the negative is the computed object. QUANTIFIER: ∀ unital `ℛ`, ∀ K-diagonal completion with `|f| < 1` (proof). SOURCE: structural (strict contractivity of dephasing + non-expansion of unital maps). VALUE: the complement is exact — a sustaining operation must be **non-unital** on the reference (population pumping: fresh preparation, Prop 6.3, or a K-rotating failure branch, Remark 4.6.2(b)), *or* the coupling must read the longitudinal component (Theorem 5.9). This is a no-go on a **declared class**, not on the existence of an action-derived refresh (NC-M68.3 stands), and not a theorem that "all refresh is impossible" (audit §7.3).

### 6.6 Theorem 6.6 (asymptotic transverse depletion under any sequence of unital refreshes) — v1.2; derived by the v1.1 auditor

**Theorem 6.6.** Let `Λ` be as in Theorem 6.5 with a fixed multiplier `|f| < 1`, let `(ℛ_n)` be **any** sequence of unital CPTP maps (possibly different at every cycle), and let `r_{n+1} = ℛ_n(Λ(r_n))`. Then
\[
|r_n|^2 - |r_{n+1}|^2 \;\ge\; (1-|f|^2)\,|P_⊥ r_n|^2,\qquad\text{hence}\qquad (1-|f|^2)\sum_{n\ge0}|P_⊥ r_n|^2 \le |r_0|^2,\qquad P_⊥ r_n \to 0 .
\]
The same conclusion holds for the order `r_{n+1} = Λ(ℛ_n(r_n))` (apply the inequality to the intermediate state `ℛ_n r_n`; then `P_⊥ r_{n+1} = f·P_⊥(ℛ_n r_n) → 0`). No uniform decay *rate* follows, and the statement needs `|f|` fixed (or `|f_n| ≤ f̄ < 1` uniformly; a strength that weakens to `f_n → 1` is outside it).

*Proof.* `|r_{n+1}|² ≤ |Λ(r_n)|² = |r_n|² − (1−|f|²)|P_⊥ r_n|²` (non-expansion of `ℛ_n`, then Theorem 4.6). Summing the telescoping inequality gives the bound; a convergent series has vanishing terms. ∎ *(C34; W56: 2 000 random rotations, the summed bound holds and the transverse component reaches machine zero.)*

*Reading.* Theorem 6.5 excludes a transverse fixed point; Theorem 6.6 is stronger — it excludes transverse periodic cycles and proves full asymptotic depletion of the transverse resource under any unital cycle model with fixed strength. It makes the no-unital result directly about *persistence*.

*Remark 6.6.1 (K15 refinement; maximal strength).* At `λ = ‖A‖` a **non-canonical failure state** with Bloch vector `v` (any `V`, unital or not) gives for the longitudinal component the exact one-step law `x' = x + ½(1−f²)(1−x)(1+v_K)` (C58). For `v_K > −1` its only fixed point is `x = 1`, whose transverse part is necessarily zero: a pure re-preparation on the failure branch **by itself** cannot sustain a transverse reference state at maximal strength; only `v_K = −1` (the canonical branch up to a phase) leaves `x` free, and only additional cycle operations can change this. "Non-unital" is thus a necessary complement of Theorems 6.5–6.6, not a sufficient physical construction.

### 6.8 Source audit against the current S14 action (seed v1.3 §12.6, carried unchanged; K12d re-typed)

The current ZS-S14 v2.1 local action types its bulk/boundary field content and leaves `V(H₅, H)` as a potential term; it does **not** explicitly type any term as a reference-state replacement, `m_J` injection, or refresh map. The `Φ_Z/H₅` relation and `ι_{ZΦ}` remain selection problems; the boundary phase law is not derived; the CTP/effective boundary completion is not fixed. Hence "S14 v2.1 explicitly supplies K12a" is **not supported**, while "no admissible action-derived K12a map can emerge from S14" is **OPEN**.

**No-Go Emission Gate — NOT PASSED, no-go NOT emitted.** SIGN: the gate asks for a positive object (a refresh map); QUANTIFIER: absence of an explicit term is a single-representation observation, not an enumeration; SOURCE: the obstacle is in an incomplete construction (open potential, unspecified effective completion), not in a frozen external source. A publishable negative theorem requires a frozen admissible action/effective-map class and an exhaustive proof over it. **K12a status: OPEN.** The S14 v2.1 text likewise does not type the resource axis of the seam coupling; **K12d status: OPEN** (neither "the action forces `w_K = 0`" nor "the action supplies `w_K ≠ 0`" is supported; a grading superselection would force `w = ±e_J ⊂ {w_K = 0}` and kill the tilted route, but the converse — that the erased class is *only* the grading-even one — is now known to be false).

## 7. Cross-paper constraints (ZS-M60 / ZS-M67), re-quantified

**Corollary 7.1 (K10 re-quantified) — soldered resource-axis class.** *Class hypothesis (governs the whole corollary, including the angle-free ceiling below; v1.4.1, audit v1.4 F1).* **Assume the coupling lies in the soldered resource-axis class `w = ±e_J`, with the original `J` grading fixed**, so that the transducer coherence of Theorem 5.8(a) is the ZS-M60 odd coherence: `T_w = T`. Suppose then that the ZS-M60 floor `T ≥ M_*` and the ZS-M67 record `|κ| = T₂` were demanded of the **same** rank-two reference (this joint demand is itself a hypothesis; the two papers do not assert it). Then `m_J² ≤ 1 − T² ≤ 1 − M_*²` and, by Theorem 5.3 with `2/(2−T²)` increasing in `T²` while `√(1−T²)` decreases,
\[
|κ_*| \le \max_{T^2 \ge M_*^2}\frac{2\sqrt{1-T^2}}{2-T^2} = \frac{2\sqrt{1-M_*^2}}{2-M_*^2} = 0.91156…\qquad (M_* = 0.763362818245963536),
\]
because `u ↦ 2√(1−u)/(2−u)` is decreasing on `[0,1)` (the bound is a supremum over `α`, not attained for `T > 0`). This does **not** exclude `T₂ = 0.8354`. The seed's `|κ_*| ≤ √(1−M_*²) = 0.6460 < T₂` (row V19) relied on `|κ_*| ≤ |m_J|` and is withdrawn. *(C20, exact rational comparison on the decimal inputs: `1 − M_*² < T₂²` and `T₂²(2−M_*²)² < 4(1−M_*²)`.)* **Within this class, K10 is no longer a kill test on a rank-two reference; it is a constraint** (`|κ_*| < 0.9116`). *Outside the class the ceiling does not hold:* for `w = e_K` and `m = p e_K` with any `p ∈ [M_*, 1)` the floor `T = p ≥ M_*` holds, `T_w = 0`, and the unique fixed point has `|κ_*| = p` faithfully **at every admissible angle**, so the general-class supremum under the floor is `1` (unattained). Corollary 5.12's own `q = 2` state supplies `p = tanh 2η = 3√5/7 = 0.95831… > 0.91156…` exactly, without mentioning any target (W69). Note that `T₂ = 0.8354 < 0.9116`, so W66 breaks the *fixed-angle exclusion*, not this ceiling; and `σ_2` does not satisfy the literal antecedent `|κ| = T₂`, so neither witness refutes the class-scoped corollary itself — what they refute is any use of the ceiling or the envelope **outside** `w = ±e_J`.

**Fixed-angle envelope and the exact kill condition — for the soldered resource-axis class `w = ±e_J` (v1.3; derived by the v1.2 auditor; class hypothesis added in v1.4 after the v1.3 audit F2).** *Hypothesis.* Throughout this paragraph the coupling is in the soldered resource-axis class `w = ±e_J` with the original `J` grading fixed, so that the transducer coherence of Theorem 5.8(a) is the M60 odd coherence, `T_w = T`. For a fixed collision angle `c = cos 2α ∈ (−1,1)` the maximal capacity over references with `T ≥ M_*` is
\[
B(c) := \max_{T \ge M_*} |κ_*| = \frac{m_{\max}(1-c)}{1-m_{\max}^2 c},\qquad m_{\max} := \sqrt{1-M_*^2} = 0.64597…,
\]
because at fixed `T` the map `m ↦ m(1−c)/(1−(1−T²)c)` is maximised by the pure reference `m = √(1−T²)`, and `m ↦ m(1−c)/(1−m²c)` is increasing (`∂_m = (1−c)(1+cm²)/(1−cm²)² > 0`), so the maximum sits at `T = M_*`. `B` is decreasing in `c` with `B(0) = m_{\max}` (the seed's bound). Hence **the joint demand is excluded iff `B(c) < T₂` iff `c > c_crit := (m_{\max} − T₂)/(m_{\max}(1 − T₂ m_{\max})) = −0.63692416…`**; at `c = c_crit` the pure reference at `T = M_*` reaches exactly `T₂`. Consequently `cos 2α ≥ 0` is a **sufficient** condition for the K10 kill (then `B(c) ≤ B(0) = 0.6460 < T₂`), **not a necessary one**: e.g. at `cos 2α = −1/10`, `B = 0.68210… < T₂` and the joint demand is still excluded (C61, exact rational comparison of squares). v1.1's "kill if `T = 0` is forced" was vacuous under `T ≥ M_* > 0` (audit v1.1 F8) and v1.2's "kill iff `cos 2α ≥ 0`" (contribution table, §10.2, claim map, session) mistook a sufficient condition for an equivalence (audit v1.2 F3; the §7 body had the correct "if"); both withdrawn. As before, this is a comparison inside the fixed-angle same-reference joint-demand hypothesis; whether the angle comes from the action is a separate question (K11: it must not be chosen).

**Class scope of the envelope (v1.4; v1.3 audit F2 — a required correction).** The envelope, `c_crit` and the exclusion criterion are statements about the **soldered class `w = ±e_J`**, and they must not be quoted for the general Clifford-pair class of Theorem 5.8. The reason is a type distinction (§2.3): the M60 floor constrains the **odd coherence** `T² = m_K² + m_{K'}²`, whereas the fixed point of `H_R` is governed by the **transducer coherence** `T_w² = |m|² − m_w²`. These are **equal as functionals on all reference states iff `w = ±e_J`** (the quadratic form `T² − T_w² = m_J² − (m·w)²` vanishes identically iff `ww^T = e_Je_J^T`; C70) — at one particular state they may coincide for other axes, which is why the class condition is a statement about the coupling and not about a state. For a tilted resource axis the functionals differ and the exclusion fails. *Exact counterexample (W66).* Take the proper frame `(u, v, w) = (e_{K'}, e_J, e_K)`, the reference `m = T₂e_K` and `α = π/4` (`c = 0 > c_crit`). Then the odd coherence is `T = T₂ > M_*`, the transducer coherence is `T_w = 0`, `σ` is invariant under the unconditional return, and a direct `4×4` partial trace gives
\[
\Phi_\sigma(\rho) = \tfrac12\big(I - T_2 J_E\big),\qquad M = 0,\qquad κ_* = -T_2,\qquad 1-|r^*|^2 = 1-T_2^2 > 0 ,
\]
so the joint demand `T ≥ M_*` **and** `|κ| = T₂` on one reference is met, faithfully and at a finite angle above `c_crit`. (Putting `T₂` into the state here is a device for breaking a universal exclusion statement, not a physical prediction or a fit; the same frame with the `A`-derived `σ_2` of Corollary 5.12 gives `T = 3√5/7 > M_*` and `|κ_*| = 3√5/7 > T₂` without mentioning the target at all.) **Consequently:** Corollary 7.1, its summary in §0/§1/§10.2 and the claim map now carry the class hypothesis; a general-class version would require a *new* hypothesis `T_w ≥ M_*`, which is a different physical assumption and cannot be imported from the M60 floor on `T`. v1.3's unqualified statements are withdrawn (Appendix D.5, F-2).
