## 8. ZS-M67 debts: the chiral-bag antiunitary lemma and the algebraic half of Corollary 38.7

### 8.1 Lemma 8.1 (`k_⊥`-dressed antiunitary symmetry; pointwise level-wise vanishing of the axial density)

**Setting (B1–B4).** `Σ³ := iγ¹γ²` (real, `= diag(σ₃, σ₃)` in B2), `𝒦` complex conjugation of the spinor components, and for `k_⊥ = |k|(cos q, sin q)`,
\[
𝒮_k := γ^0\, e^{-iφ_k Σ^3/2}\,𝒦,\qquad φ_k := 2q − π .
\]

**Lemma 8.1.** (a) `𝒮_k` is antiunitary on `L²(ℝ₊;ℂ⁴)`, position-preserving, and satisfies `𝒮_k² = +1`.
(b) `𝒮_k H_k 𝒮_k⁻¹ = H_k` for every real `m(x₃)`, `μ(x₃)` and every `θ_m` (as an identity of differential expressions); `𝒮_k B_θ 𝒮_k⁻¹ = B_θ`, hence `𝒮_k L_θ = L_θ`, for every `θ`; and `𝒮_k D_θ = D_θ`, so `𝒮_k` commutes with the self-adjoint operator `(H_k, D_θ)` for bounded real profiles.
(c) `𝒮_k γ₅ 𝒮_k⁻¹ = −γ₅`, and pointwise `(𝒮_kφ)(x₃)^†γ₅(𝒮_kφ)(x₃) = −φ(x₃)^†γ₅φ(x₃)` for every spinor field `φ`.
(d) Consequently every non-degenerate `L²`-eigenmode `φ_n` of `(H_k, D_θ)` has `φ_n(x₃)^†γ₅φ_n(x₃) = 0` **pointwise in `x₃`** (and in `x_⊥`, by translation invariance), and every finite-dimensional degenerate eigenspace has pointwise vanishing `γ₅`-trace `Σ_n φ_n(x₃)^†γ₅φ_n(x₃) = 0` over any orthonormal basis.
(e) At `k_⊥ = 0`, `𝒮 = γ⁰e^{−iφΣ³/2}𝒦` is a symmetry for **every** `φ`.

*Proof.* (a) `𝒦` is antiunitary and `γ⁰e^{−iφΣ³/2}` is unitary, so `𝒮_k` is antiunitary; both factors act pointwise in `x₃`. `𝒮_k² = γ⁰R\,\overline{γ⁰R} = γ⁰Rγ⁰R̄` with `R := e^{−iφ_kΣ³/2}`; `Σ³` is real and commutes with `γ⁰`, so `R̄ = e^{+iφ_kΣ³/2} = R⁻¹` and `γ⁰Rγ⁰R⁻¹ = I`.

(b) Write `H_k = α¹k₁ + α²k₂ + α³p₃ + β m(x₃)e^{iθ_mγ₅} + μ(x₃)`, `α^i = γ⁰γ^i`, `β = γ⁰`, `p₃ = −i∂₃`. Conjugation by `𝒦`: `α¹, α³, β, γ₅` are real (B2), `α²` is imaginary, and `p₃ ↦ −p₃` (`∂₃` is real, `i ↦ −i`); so `𝒦H_k𝒦 = α¹k₁ − α²k₂ − α³p₃ + βm e^{−iθ_mγ₅} + μ`. Conjugation by `γ⁰` flips every `α^i` (`{β, α^i} = 0`) and fixes `β`; on the mass term `γ⁰(β e^{−iθ_mγ₅})γ⁰ = β e^{+iθ_mγ₅}` because `γ⁰γ₅γ⁰ = −γ₅`. Hence
`γ⁰𝒦 H_k 𝒦γ⁰ = −α¹k₁ + α²k₂ + α³p₃ + βm e^{iθ_mγ₅} + μ`: the mass, potential and normal-derivative terms are already restored and the tangential term has been reflected, `(k₁, k₂) ↦ (−k₁, k₂)`. Finally `R = e^{−iφΣ³/2}` commutes with `β, γ₅, α³` and rotates `(α¹, α²)` in its plane by the angle `φ` (with the orientation fixed by `Σ³ = iγ¹γ²`); the composition "reflect across the 2-axis, then rotate by `φ`" fixes the vector at polar angle `q` iff `π − q + φ ≡ q (mod 2π)`, i.e. `φ = 2q − π`. *(C21: the identity `𝒮_kH_k𝒮_k⁻¹ = H_k` is verified symbolically in `q, |k|, m, θ_m, μ, p₃`, treating `p₃` as an imaginary-odd symbol; it is exact, so `m, μ` may be arbitrary real functions of `x₃`.)* For `B_θ = cos θ(iγ³) + sin θ(γ³γ₅)`: `𝒦` flips the sign of the first term (`γ³` real, `i ↦ −i`) and fixes the second (real); `γ⁰` flips `γ³` and fixes `γ³γ₅`; `R` commutes with both. So `𝒮_kB_θ𝒮_k⁻¹ = B_θ` and `𝒮_k` maps `L_θ = ker(B_θ − 1)` onto itself, for every `θ`. Domain: `𝒮_k` is a constant invertible antilinear matrix acting pointwise, so it preserves `H¹(ℝ₊;ℂ⁴)`, and it preserves the boundary subspace; hence `𝒮_kD_θ = D_θ`. For bounded real `m, μ`, `(H_k, D_θ)` is self-adjoint (the free chiral-bag operator is self-adjoint by M67 Theorems 1 and 3, and `βm e^{iθ_mγ₅} + μ` is a bounded symmetric perturbation, Kato–Rellich), and the identity (b) on the common domain says `𝒮_k` commutes with it.

(c) `𝒦` fixes `γ₅` (real) and `γ⁰` anticommutes with it. For the density, `(𝒮_kφ)^†γ₅(𝒮_kφ) = (γ⁰Rφ̄)^†γ₅(γ⁰Rφ̄) = φ^{\mathsf T}R^†γ⁰γ₅γ⁰Rφ̄ = −φ^{\mathsf T}γ₅φ̄ = −\overline{φ^†γ₅φ} = −φ^†γ₅φ`, since `R` commutes with `γ₅` and `φ^†γ₅φ` is real. *(C22.)*

(d) If `H_kφ_n = E_nφ_n` with `φ_n ∈ D_θ`, then by (b) `𝒮_kφ_n ∈ D_θ` and `H_k𝒮_kφ_n = E_n𝒮_kφ_n`. If `E_n` is non-degenerate, `𝒮_kφ_n = cφ_n` with `|c| = 1` (antiunitarity), and (c) gives `φ_n^†γ₅φ_n = |c|²φ_n^†γ₅φ_n = −φ_n^†γ₅φ_n`, hence `0` pointwise. If the eigenspace `V_E` is degenerate with orthonormal basis `{φ_n}`, then `{𝒮_kφ_n}` is another orthonormal basis of `V_E` (antiunitary maps preserve orthonormality and, by (b), `V_E`); the pointwise trace `Σ_n φ_n(x)^†γ₅φ_n(x) = tr[γ₅ P_E(x,x)]` with `P_E(x,x) = Σ_nφ_n(x)φ_n(x)^†` depends only on `V_E`, so it equals `Σ_n(𝒮_kφ_n)^†γ₅(𝒮_kφ_n) = −Σ_nφ_n^†γ₅φ_n`, hence `0`.

(e) At `k_⊥ = 0` the tangential term is absent and every `R = e^{−iφΣ³/2}` commutes with the remaining terms. *(C22.)* ∎

**Scope and known breakers.** (i) *Profiles:* the intertwining identities are exact for arbitrary real measurable `m, μ`; the self-adjoint-operator statement is asserted for bounded profiles (scope correction S-5). (ii) *Tangential vector potential* `A_⊥(x₃)`: the lemma holds verbatim with `k_⊥ ↦ k_⊥ − A_⊥` if the **direction** of `k_⊥ − A_⊥(x₃)` is independent of `x₃`; if it varies, the required rotation angle depends on `x₃`, `R(φ(x₃))` no longer commutes with `α³∂₃`, and the lemma does not apply (breaker (a)). (iii) *Normal component* `A₃(x₃)α³` is `𝒮_k`-odd (breaker (b); C23) — but it is pure gauge in one dimension and removable. (iv) *Polarisation-mixing perturbations* not of the real-scalar/real-mass form break the symmetry (breaker (c); the lattice control W24 raises the density to `2×10⁻³`). (v) *Continuum vs lattice:* the Wilson-regularised half-space operator (real Wilson term `∝ γ⁰`, hopping `∓iγ⁰γ³`) satisfies the same identities, since the added terms are real multiples of `γ⁰` and `γ⁰γ³`; W24 exhibits `𝒮H𝒮⁻¹ = H` to `5×10⁻¹⁶` and pointwise densities `< 3×10⁻¹⁴` on all 158 admissible levels of a 40-site chain with `x`-dependent real mass and potential and bag angle `θ = 0.9`. (vi) *Dimension:* the theorem is stated in 3+1 with a 2+1 boundary (B1); the construction uses only that the tangential Clifford pair `(γ¹, γ²)` has one real and one imaginary member and that `Σ³` is real. A 2+1 *reduction* inherits the lemma only if it retains a compatible four-component (reducible) Clifford representation with the same `γ₅` structure; an irreducible `2×2` representation in 2+1 dimensions has no such `γ₅`, and a 2+1 half-space has only one tangential direction — outside the statement (audit v1.2 F8). **(vii) *Spectral regulators (scope corrected in v1.2; v1.0 audit F7, v1.1 audit §6):*** (d) is a statement about `L²`-eigenmodes and finite-dimensional degenerate eigenspaces. Its extension to a regularised density `𝒜_f(x₃) := tr[γ₅ f(H_k)(x₃,x₃)]` holds for those real spectral functions `f` for which `f(H_k)` has a **continuous Hermitian integral kernel** `K_f(x,y)` at fixed `k_⊥` (so that the diagonal `K_f(x,x)` is defined pointwise; local trace-class alone is not the same as a canonical pointwise diagonal — audit v1.2 F8): the antiunitary functional calculus gives `K_f(x,y) = U\overline{K_f(x,y)}U^†` with `U = γ⁰e^{−iφ_kΣ³/2}`, so at `x = y` the real number `d = tr[γ₅K_f(x,x)]` satisfies `d = −\bar d`, hence `d = 0`. *One concrete class (route supplied by the v1.2 auditor; stated, not re-proved here):* real Borel `f` with `sup_E (1+E²)|f(E)| < ∞` — then `T := |f(H_k)|^{1/2}` maps `L²` into `D(H_k)`, whose graph norm is equivalent to `H¹` for bounded potentials, `H¹(ℝ₊) ↪ C` with evaluation continuous in operator norm, and `K_f(x,y) = T_x\,\mathrm{sign}(f(H_k))\,T_y^*` is a continuous Hermitian kernel; this includes the fixed-`k_⊥` Gaussian regulator `e^{−tH_k²}`, `t > 0`. The statement for the full half-space density additionally requires the `k_⊥`-integral to be justified for that `f` (an obligation, not discharged here). Regulators without a pointwise kernel diagonal (e.g. `f ≡ 1`, delta-kernel) are **outside** the statement, and the continuous spectrum is covered only through such kernels, not through "eigenmodes". v1.0/v1.1 wrote "every spectral regulator `f(H)`"; that quantifier is withdrawn.

**Consequence for ZS-M67.** Observation 38.6 (`[열림]` in M67 v3.4.1) becomes Lemma 8.1 (`[검증됨]`, mathematics, in the scope above). M67 §22.7's sentence "no pointwise unitary or antiunitary symmetry of the required type exists … the antiunitary system is inconsistent" is **incorrect as stated**: the missing ingredient there was the `k_⊥`-dependent normal-axis rotation `e^{−iφ_kΣ³/2}` (correction proposal M67 v3.4.2, pending user acceptance; not applied here). Lemma 8.1 is stronger than M67 Theorem 38.3 in the sense of scope (vii): it extends the vanishing from `f(H²)`-regularised densities to real regulators `f(H)` with kernel diagonal at fixed `k_⊥`.

**Prior art (§12, E1).** Asorey–Balachandran–Pérez-Pardo (JHEP 12 (2013) 073) observe that time-reversal and parity invariance of edge Hamiltonians "emerge for adapted geometry"; chiral-bag edge spectra and `η`-relations (Fresneda–de Souza–Vassilevich 2023) and the hedgehog-bag baryon-number line (Goldstone–Jaffe) concern global spectral asymmetry, not a pointwise density. The fixed-`k_⊥`, all-`θ`, real-profile, **pointwise** combination was `NOT_FOUND` in the swept family (abstract/snippet level, two query families). Status: mathematics `[검증됨]`; novelty `OPEN-NOVELTY`; no novelty adjective is used.

### 8.2 Proposition 8.2 (Corollary 38.7 of ZS-M67, algebraic closure)

**Proposition 8.2.** (i) `e^{iαγ₅}B_θe^{−iαγ₅} = B_{θ+2α}`, hence `e^{iαγ₅}L_θ = L_{θ+2α}`. (ii) Let `α ∈ W^{1,∞}(ℝ₊)` (bounded and Lipschitz, `α' ∈ L^∞`). Then the local axial rotation `ψ ↦ e^{iα(x)γ₅}ψ` maps the chiral-bag domain `D_θ` to itself iff `α|_{x₃=0} ∈ πℤ`. (iii) Under (ii) and for a spectral regulator `f(H)` of the fixed-domain operator within scope (vii) of Lemma 8.1, the Fujikawa exponent `∫α(x)𝒜_f(x)d³x` vanishes, because `𝒜_f ≡ 0` pointwise (Lemma 8.1(d)/(vii) for `f(H)`; M67 Theorem 38.3 for `f(H²)`).

*Proof.* (i) `B_θ = ie^{iθγ₅}γ³` (M67 Thm 3(iv)) and `γ³e^{−iαγ₅} = e^{iαγ₅}γ³`, so `e^{iαγ₅}B_θe^{−iαγ₅} = ie^{i(θ+2α)γ₅}γ³ = B_{θ+2α}` *(C23)*. (ii) `L_{θ+2α(0)} = L_θ` iff `2α(0) ∈ 2πℤ`. For `α ∈ W^{1,∞}`, `∂₃(e^{iαγ₅}ψ) = iα'γ₅e^{iαγ₅}ψ + e^{iαγ₅}∂₃ψ` with `‖α'γ₅e^{iαγ₅}ψ‖ ≤ ‖α'‖_∞‖ψ‖`, so multiplication by `e^{iαγ₅}` is bounded on `H¹` with bounded inverse (`−α`), and the boundary trace is continuous, giving both directions. Boundedness and smoothness of `α` alone are **not** sufficient: `α = sin x₃²` is smooth and bounded with `α'` unbounded, and for `ψ = (1+x₃)^{−0.6}χ ∈ H¹` the product `α'ψ ∉ L²`, so `e^{iαγ₅}ψ ∉ H¹` (W39, numerical growth of `‖(e^{iα}ψ)'‖_{L²[0,L]}` with `L`). (iii) immediate. ∎

*Status.* (i) `[검증됨]` (algebra); (ii) `[검증됨]` under `α ∈ W^{1,∞}` (v1.0 stated it for "smooth bounded `α`", which is false — corrected in v1.1). The identification of (iii) with the path-integral Jacobian of the Z-Spin boundary theory remains `[가설]` (it presupposes M67's regularisation and A1–A4 of M67); the local-`α(x)` domain-preservation obligation of M67 Cor. 38.7 is discharged **exactly** by the condition `α|_∂ ∈ πℤ`, and for `α|_∂ ∉ πℤ` the rotation changes the boundary condition; M67 Thm 38.4 (co-rotation) covers the **constant-`α`** boundary-space rotation algebra — a variable `α(x)` produces derivative terms in the conjugated operator, so the quantum Jacobian of an arbitrary local domain-changing rotation is *not* "already handled" upstream and remains part of the open physical question (audit v1.2 F8; v1.2's wording corrected). The v1.2 auditor also notes a sign slip in M67 §38's prose (`U` said to map `L_θ` to `L_{θ−2α}` while the displayed `U^†` relation carries the correct sign); (i) here uses the displayed sign — an upstream correction-proposal item.

## 9. Physical interpretation and the bridge (conditional)

### 9.1 Eight-item bridge check (MANUSCRIPT §6.3)

|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | typed carrier | reference fibre `ℂ²` with `(K, K', J)`: DERIVED from `(A, J)` | M67 carrier `E₊`: IMPORTED (M67 Thm 19) |
| 2 | action-derived operator/channel | the transfer `σ ↦ AσA/tr` is the holonomy; its status as the return **map on states** (A3) is HYPOTHESIS; the instrument completion A3′ is a canonical choice (Lüders), not derived | `H_R` (frame, `α`, `χ`) is a construction, not action-derived: OPEN (K12a/K12d) |
| 3 | representation/intertwiner | `ι₀`: DERIVED | soldering onto `(J_E, X_E, −Y_E)`: DERIVED |
| 4 | normalisation/measure | trace renormalisation of a filtering map: DERIVED given A3; branch weighting (S6/Born): OPEN | Kraus/partial trace: DERIVED |
| 5 | state/environment | reference state `σ`, `m_K(0)`, `m_J`: OPEN (origin) | fresh independent copies (A5): HYPOTHESIS; the `E₊ ⊗ R` subsystem/dilation that makes the reduced channel available (A5, K17): HYPOTHESIS — M67 treats the boundary carrier as a pushforward of the bulk state, so it does not follow from `ι₀`; back-action on a re-used reference: OPEN |

Two or more load-bearing items are OPEN/HYPOTHESIS in each column; therefore **every physical statement in this paper is conditional**, and the paper's OUTPUT ROLE is SUPPORT+METHOD. The bridge ladder (audit v1.1 §3): declared holonomy → conditional return law (verified); prescribed filter → a canonical complete instrument (construction verified); prescribed reference and coupling → limiting fixed-point record (verified under `sin 2α ≠ 0`); changing reference → actual carrier limit (verified **inside** the fresh-copy model, Lemma 5.10); Z-action → actual coupling, instrument, state, readout (OPEN); record → Born weighting / single outcome (OPEN); return count → physical clock/metric (OPEN).

### 9.2 What "the reference returns" assumes

A3 says the reference is the fibre and is transported by the holonomy between collisions. If an alternative construction shielded the reference from the return, that shielding would itself be an action-derived object to exhibit (§10.1). The held-reference reading of §5.4 additionally assumes that the collision channel relaxes (rate set by the eigenvalues of Lemma 5.10, i.e. by `cos 2α`) before the next return changes `σ`; near `α → π/2`, where the amplification of Theorem 5.3 is largest, the relaxation is slowest (`det(I−M) ∝ sin²2α`), so the two conditions pull against each other. *v1.2:* this trade-off is now partly quantified — the carrier record follows the reference with the transient of Prop 5.11 and converges to the limiting capacity by Lemma 5.10 — but only within A5's fresh-copy model; v1.1's claim that the unconditional linear law "gives the lifetime of a changing record directly" is withdrawn (Prop 5.11). In the conditional reading the tilted-coupling limiting record of Theorem 5.9 is evaluated at the return-invariant limit state, where "held reference" is exact in the limit, and Lemma 5.10 supplies the convergence to it.

### 9.3 The instrument reading

A3′ replaces "the reference is filtered" by "the reference is measured by the holonomy": the return is a two-outcome (Lüders) instrument whose success branch is the boost and whose failure branch is the negative null state. Physically, the reference either survives the loop with its transverse populations boosted away (conditional) or is absorbed into the negative light-cone state; unconditionally the loop is a perfect light-cone-basis dephaser at `φ⁻⁴`. Which reading applies is not decided here: it requires the action-derived instrument (which branch is physically recorded, with what probability weighting) — the same S6/Born item that ZS-M67 leaves open — and the instrument's strength and failure branch are free (Remark 4.6.2). Theorem 4.7 guarantees that the two readings are consistent on the reference: post-selected retention and survival probability multiply to `φ^{−4n}` exactly.

### 9.4 Z-Spin specificity of the mechanism (v1.3; audit v1.2 §6)

The filtering, dephasing and collision mechanisms of §4–§6 are not specific to `tr A = 3`. For any hyperbolic `A_t = e^{η_tK}` with `tr A_t = t > 2`, `cosh η_t = t/2`, the canonical instrument is `E₀ = A_t/e^{η_t}`, `E₁ = √(1−e^{−4η_t})P₋`, its unconditional channel is K-dephasing with `m_K` invariant and transverse factor
\[
f_t = e^{-2η_t} = \Big(\frac{t-\sqrt{t^2-4}}{2}\Big)^2 ,
\]
and every theorem of §4–§6 holds with `φ⁻⁴ ↦ f_t`, `2η ↦ 2η_t` (C65, symbolic in `t`); `t = 3` gives `f₃ = φ⁻⁴` and `2η = 2 arcosh(3/2)`. What is Z-Spin-specific is the **input** (the trace-three reversible class, the chiral-bag carrier and grading, the locked comparison constants), not the mechanism. Accordingly the paper is a **trace-three specialisation of general qubit filtering/collision phenomena** whose defensible centre is the exact coherent-reference transducer with its survival and convergence classification; the claim that the Z-action *selects* this class and instrument is a separate, open statement (§6.7, NC-M68.1/7/8). This is consistent with the absence of a CORE claim.

### 9.5 The `2η` per return

`2η = 2 arcosh(3/2) = 1.9248…` is the rapidity added per return. It is a conjugacy invariant of the trace-three class and is target-blind. It becomes a rate only after an action-derived return duration `Δτ` exists (K12c), and Mission §1.3 forbids reading `Δτ` from `arg λ` or from the holonomy phase without a mapping. Nothing in this paper supplies `Δτ`; the RQ2 advance claimed here is the quantitative fibre/return asset (exact rapidity per return, exact reference-resource law), not a clock or metric bridge (audit v1.1 §3).

## 11. Conclusion, breakthrough trace, and the author-side referee pass

### 11.1 Conclusion

The reversible trace-three holonomy soldered onto the chiral-bag carrier generates a record (Theorem 5.2: unique fixed point for `sin 2α ≠ 0`, faithful except for the pure-diagonal reference by Theorem 5.7, value `−χ m_w g`, sign = chirality) and — for every coupling whose resource axis lies in the transverse plane, `w_K = 0`, a class that contains the joint-grading-even soldered coupling and more — erases it: conditionally as a boost of rapidity `2η` per return (Theorems 4.3–4.5, ceilings `sech((N−1)η)`), unconditionally as exact light-cone dephasing at `φ⁻⁴` per return (Theorem 4.6, the reference-resource decay law, which is the Lüders completion of the effect `e^{−2η}A²`). The two readings are tied on the reference by (probability) × (retention) `= φ^{−4n}` (Theorem 4.7). A coherent reference can amplify a transverse record by at most `2/(2−T²)` (Theorem 5.3, non-strict, supremum not attained for `T > 0`), which is why the seed's one-return exclusion of `T₂` fails and the correct capacity exclusion is at three returns (Corollary 5.4). In the erased class no unital refresh — fixed or varying from cycle to cycle, the exact involutive cycle `Ad_J∘ℋ` of Theorem 6.4 included (it keeps the record only in its post-selected branch at `φ⁻⁴` per cycle) — sustains a transverse resource unconditionally (Theorems 6.5–6.6): a sustaining operation must pump the transverse population, and a pure re-preparation on the failure branch alone does not suffice at maximal strength (Remark 6.6.1). But the holonomy conserves (unconditionally) or saturates (conditionally) the **longitudinal** light-cone population, and a coupling whose resource axis has a light-cone component reads exactly that: for `0 < |w_K| < 1` the limiting record is biased and faithful in the conditional reading, and biased and faithful iff `0 < |w_K m_K(0)| < 1` in the unconditional one (Theorem 5.9); in the conditional model a target `ρ` is attained at an admissible angle iff `|w_K| > x(ρ)` (strict, exact); and the actual fresh-copy collision process reaches these limiting records (Lemma 5.10; the relaxation is Schur-stable but not one-step contractive), with a carrier lifetime that is not the `φ⁻⁴` reference law (Proposition 5.11). For the soldered coupling the two-return persistence of a `T₂`-magnitude capacity is attained at a finite angle (`16/19`, Cor 5.4(iv)), and — **within the soldered resource-axis class `w = ±e_J`, where the transducer coherence is the M60 odd coherence** — the K10 joint demand is excluded at a fixed angle iff `cos 2α > c_crit = −0.6369…` (Cor 7.1 envelope); outside that class it is not excluded at all (W66). The surviving longitudinal branch is not exotic: the holonomy's own invariant references `A^q/tr A^q` are read faithfully by a resource axis along `K`, with record `−χ tanh(qη)` at every admissible angle (Corollary 5.12), so the reversal-blindness identity `Tr f(A)J = 0` closes the `J`-population route only. None of the mechanism is specific to `tr A = 3` (§9.4): the Z-Spin content is the input, and the selection of coupling, state, instrument and reference supply by the action remains open. What a physical record on this route requires is therefore, within the declared model and per reading: conditionally a resource axis with `0 < |w_K| < 1`; unconditionally a resource axis and an initial longitudinal population with `0 < |w_K m_K(0)| < 1` (which allows `|w_K| = 1`, where `κ_∞ = −χ w_K m_K(0)`, and is empty for `m_K(0) = 0`); **or** a transverse-resource coupling (`w_K = 0`, erased in both readings) with a non-unital action-derived refresh — and, in every case, the action derivation of the coupling frame, the collision angle and chirality, the reference state and its supply, the instrument and the readout, none of which is fixed by `w_K` (§6.7), together with the subsystem/dilation that makes the reduced collision channel available at all (A5, K17). Along the way, ZS-M67 Observation 38.6 becomes Lemma 8.1 (pointwise, for eigenmodes and kernel-diagonal regulators) and the domain-preservation obligation of its Corollary 38.7 is discharged exactly under `α ∈ W^{1,∞}`.

### 11.3 Referee pass (author-side; not an independent audit — the same-lineage limit 2/2 was reached at v1.1, so this pass is restricted to mechanical re-verification of the audit items, §B)

1. *Restatement of an existing theorem?* Theorem 4.3: Han–Kim–Noz specialised; Theorem 4.6: Lüders operation ([BS98]) with fixed points = commutant ([LW10]), specialised; Theorem 5.2 at `T = 0`: thermal-ancilla short-collision result; Theorem 5.8(a): elementary covariance; Theorem 6.5: unital contractivity ([PWPR06]) + dephasing; Lemma 5.10: Jury criterion — all typed. Theorems 4.4–4.5, 5.3, 5.7, 5.9, Lemma 5.10's application, Prop 5.11: not found; OPEN-NOVELTY.
2. *Drop one assumption and it is false?* Drop A5's subsystem/dilation (K17): §5–§6 are algebra on a formal tensor product. Drop the class hypothesis `w = ±e_J` in Cor 7.1: both the fixed-angle exclusion (W66) and the angle-free ceiling (W69) are false. Drop the ∀-reference quantifier in `T_w = T`: the statement fails pointwise (C70). Drop A3/A3′: §4 is algebra without physics. Drop `sin 2α ≠ 0`: no unique fixed point, every `J_E`-diagonal carrier state fixed (C49). Drop `m_K(0) > −1`: Thm 5.9(i) limit is the other null state (stated). Drop `|w_K| < 1` in the conditional reading: the limiting state is pure (W47) — but not unconditionally (C60). Drop `m_K(0) ≠ 0` unconditionally: no bias (W47, C60). Drop the adapted norm in Lemma 5.10(b): one-step contraction fails (W59). Drop A5's independence: Lemma 5.10 fails (K16). Drop `α ∈ W^{1,∞}`: Prop 8.2(ii) fails (W39). Drop the K-diagonality of the completion: Thms 6.5–6.6 fail (K15). Drop the kernel-diagonal condition: Lemma 8.1(vii) is undefined.
3. *Sample used as proof?* W15b, W16, W18, W29, W35, W39, W45–W47, W56, V17, V38, V53, V57, W24 are typed W/V; every theorem has a written proof and, where formalisable, a symbolic row; the written-only halves are declared (D43). C03's polynomial claim is a written argument with finite-`n` witness rows.
4. *Formal→physical jump?* §9.1 bridge ladder; §9.3 refuses to choose a reading; NC-M68.7–10.
5. *Hidden fitted choice?* `w_K`, frame, `α`, `χ`, `m_K(0)`, `q`, `λ`, `V`, reading, reference supply — all declared (§10.1); `T₂`, `M_*` appear only in the eight comparison rows C19, C20, C61, C62, V57, W64, W66, W69, which G40 checks against the ledger (v1.4 said "C19/C20/V57", a stale list; audit v1.4 F4). W66 puts `T₂` into a state to break a universal exclusion, and W69/C67 give the same conclusion with a target-independent `p`.
6. *Method chosen after seeing the target?* Theorem 5.9 was derived from the covariance and the conserved component; the `x(T₂)` threshold was computed afterwards from the already-frozen `x(ρ)`, and the strictness of the threshold was forced by the audit's gap identity, not by the target.
7. *Stronger alternative?* A grading-superselection theorem for the seam coupling would force `w = ±e_J` and kill the tilted route; a proof that the action forces `w_K = 0` by any other mechanism would do the same — both listed as failure modes.
8. *Finite extended to continuum?* Lemma 8.1 scope (vii) now bounds the regulator statement.
9. *`NOT_FOUND` used as absence?* No: K12d is OPEN in every direction (§6.8).
10. *Title/abstract/conclusion stronger than the body?* Four consecutive audits found a version of this defect: v1.2 in the *readings*, v1.3 in the *class scope* of the summaries, and v1.4 in the **first sentence of the corollary itself and in the stored row claim strings** — v1.4 had put the class hypothesis in the paragraph that derives the envelope, so it did not govern the angle-free ceiling above it, nor `C20`/`C61`, which a downstream reader extracts alone (F1). In v1.4.1 the hypothesis is the corollary's opening sentence and is inside both claim strings, and the general-class statement follows immediately with two witnesses (W66 for the fixed-angle exclusion, W69 for the ceiling). Recorded as a DO-NOT-REPEAT: *a new row that describes a counterexample does not repair the unqualified claim string of an old row*. The residual risk is unchanged and stated: no row checks that a sentence repeats the hypotheses of the theorem it summarises.

**Strongest objection** (carried, and sharpened by the v1.3 audit): *"a stable record obtained by inserting a reference, a coupling and an instrument that the action did not select is not a derivation of a measurement selector — and Corollary 5.12, in which the reference is even manufactured by the holonomy, does not change that."* *Disposition:* **accepted; bounded by scope.** The paper supplies candidate structures, their exact costs and their survival conditions; it does not complete the selection chain (§6.7, NC-M68.1/7/8/10; OUTPUT ROLE SUPPORT+METHOD). The v1.1 "axis relabelling" objection is subsumed: which component is read is a physical datum the paper does not supply, and the erased class is larger than the relabelling picture suggested (W45).

It is included in this package, and `provenance_missing: []` in a manifest is a statement about the generating environment, not about what a recipient received.

### B.2 Result, and what it does and does not certify

69 rows · 69 PASS · 0 FAIL · 0 SKIP · exit 0 (run with the manuscript path and `--strict`, so G42 is exercised and SKIP = 0 is required); census C=45 (40 flagged `universal`), V=4, W=15, G=4, D=1; P=0. Same-machine re-run: ledger byte-identical (B.3). Hashes are generated into `release_manifest_v1_4_1.json` and injected into the front-matter banner by `make_manifest_v1_4_1.py`, never typed by hand; `make_manifest_v1_4_1.py --check` then verifies that the injected banner hashes equal the actual script/ledger hashes (this is the hash check that G42 does *not* perform). Note the limits the v1.4 auditor stated for that command: it checks the banner against the ledger, **not** the existence and hashes of every object the manifest lists; the auditor computed those separately, and this package re-delivers the one provenance object that was missing from the v1.4 upload.

**What the ledger certifies:** that each stated identity or instance holds in the named environment. **What it does not certify:** the written proofs (the non-expansion half of Thms 6.5–6.6, the general-`R` half of Thm 5.8(a), the adapted-norm perturbation argument of Lemma 5.10(b), the domain argument of Prop 8.2(ii), the continuous-kernel scope of Lemma 8.1(vii)), the physical readings, or the paper. The v1.2 audit found that a 56/56 PASS coexisted with S2 defects in **summaries** and **evidence typing**; the **v1.3 audit found the same pattern at 63/63** in *class scope*; and the **v1.4 audit found its residue at 66/66** — the class hypothesis had reached the paragraph that derives the envelope but not the first sentence of the corollary, nor the stored claim strings of `C20`/`C61`, which is what a downstream reader extracts (audit v1.4 F1). The lesson recorded as a DO-NOT-REPEAT: *adding a new row that describes a counterexample does not repair the unqualified claim string of an old row.* v1.4.1's answer is (i) the hypothesis written into the corollary's first sentence and into both stored claim strings, (ii) a row for the angle-free ceiling as well (W69) and for the quantifier (C70), and (iii) for the one property that a prose declaration got wrong twice, a guard with a kill condition (G69). `universal` flags count free-parameter algebra checks, **not** universal theorem proofs (C09 checks `N = 2, 3`; C12 a three-level environment; C34 the norm identity; C03 `n ≤ 7`; C65 the instrument at symbolic `t`, not the §5–§6 theorems at general `t`).

**Census and evidence-basis record (VERIFY §2.2).** *C32:* the v1.2 row was described as EXACT/universal C, but its `or all(...)` clause allowed a three-sample numerical fallback and, in the audited run, that fallback decided the PASS (the symbolic branch was False); the v1.2 auditor's conservative census was therefore C36/V5/W11/G3/D1, and the auditor's injected `f_bad = f + 1000(λ−e^{η})(λ−3)(λ−5)(λ−20)/λ⁶` passed the v1.2 row while changing `f(4)` from `0.8151` to `6.2134`. v1.3's C32 has no numerical clause: the radicand identity `(1−at)(1−bt) = 1−7t+t²` with `a = e^{2η}`, `b = e^{−2η}` (`ab = 1`, `a+b = 7`, exact radical forms) closes the equivalence of the two parametrisations as principal roots, and `f_bad` now fails the row (live-fire (g)). Class C is retained on that basis. *C31:* claim string re-worded to the reference-rapidity identity it tests. *C54:* the self-cancelling conjunct is replaced by a check against the C15 supremum form; claim unchanged. *C19/C20/C61/C62/W64/W66/W69:* exact rational comparisons of the locked decimals (class C/W; the arithmetic is certified, the physical provenance of the constants is not). No class letter changed between v1.2 and v1.3, nor between v1.3 and v1.4. **v1.4 evidence-basis changes:** *C32* — the endpoint conjunct's `or bool(sp.N(...))` clause is removed; the exact branch (`1 − 7φ⁻⁴ + φ⁻⁸ = 0`) alone decides it, so no conjunct of a class-C row can be settled numerically (audit v1.3 F7). *C60* — the direct `4×4` witness is now run on **both** axes `w = ±e_K`, and the symbolic identity `κ_∞ = −χ w_K m_K(0)` is checked at `w_K = ±1` (the v1.3 row tested only `+1`, which is why it could not see the missing sign in the prose; F3). *C63* — the propagator identity is now `U = −χ Z⊗Z` per chirality instead of an `or` over the two signs (F5). *W66/C67/C68* are new (F2/F1/F4). **v1.4.1 evidence-basis changes (audit v1.4):** *C20* and *C61* — the soldered-class hypothesis `w = ±e_J` is written into the stored claim strings; the arithmetic is unchanged (F1). *C34* — `sp.N(φ⁻⁸) < 1` replaced by `φ⁻⁸ = (47−21√5)/2` with `47² > 21²·5 > 45²` (F2). *C61* — `|B(c_crit) − T₂| < 1e−28` replaced by the symbolic identity `B(c_crit) = ρ` plus the exact rational comparisons that place `c_crit` in `(−1,0)`; the decimal survives only as a display value (F2). *W69*, *C70* and the guard *G69* are new; no class letter changed, and the census moves only by the three added rows.

**What live-fire still cannot catch.** Every case above injects a defect into a *row*. The two S2 findings of the v1.3 audit lived in *prose whose row was correct*: no mutation of `C61` can reveal that §7's summary dropped `w = ±e_J`, and no mutation of `C03` can reveal that §6.7 over-read Lemma 3.3. Guard growth is not the countermeasure (kernel §10-5); the countermeasure is that each such defect now has a row whose claim string states the scope, so the next summary that drops it contradicts a published row.

Live-fire remains an **external** log rather than embedded regression rows (a lineage regression noted since seed v1.2; deposit-time debt, unchanged).

### B.4 Lineage rescan (VERIFY §7.3) and ledger provenance delta (VERIFY §11; computed, not asserted)

`v1.4 → v1.4.1`: **carried unchanged 60**, **retyped/replaced 6** (C20, C34, C61, G40, G41, G42 — claim string or evidential basis changed as recorded in B.2 and the script header; the count and the list are computed by the manifest generator, not typed), **added 3** (W69, C70, G69), **removed 0**. `60 + 6 + 3 = 69`. (For reference, `v1.3 → v1.4` was 57 carried, 6 retyped, 3 added, 0 removed; `v1.2 → v1.3` was 50/6/7/0.) The delta is computed by `make_manifest_v1_4_1.py` against the v1.4 ledger, not asserted (VERIFY §11).

Guard lineage: the v1.2 G42 was described as implementing CPN11, but the v1.2 auditor's isolated guard probe showed it PASSED a manuscript with a stale run command, a wrong census count and a wrong script hash — its scope was overstated. v1.3's G42 states its scope exactly (tokens, script name, row count, stale banner/run-command tokens, banner census = ledger census) and now fails the stale-command case (live-fire (j)) and the wrong-census case; hash consistency is the manifest generator's job (`--check`), not a row's. This is the fourth guard/witness-scope repair in the lineage (G26 → G41/G42 → W35 → G42 scope) and is recorded as a DO-NOT-REPEAT: *state a guard's scope by what it opens and compares; never name a checklist item (CPN11) that the guard only partly implements*. **v1.4 rescan (VERIFY §7.3), and why it was not enough.** The v1.3 audit's F7 identified a residual `or bool(sp.N(...))` inside `C32`; v1.4 removed it and then **declared in prose** that no `sp.N`, `float` or tolerance term appears in the PASS decision of any `C` row. The v1.4 audit read the actual code and found that declaration false: `C34` still decided part of its PASS by `sp.N(phi**−8) < 1` and `C61` by `abs(B(c_crit) − T₂) < 1e−28` (audit v1.4 F2). Both were **AND** conjuncts — not the `C32`-style numerical *escape route* that can rescue a failed exact test — so no result changed; what was wrong was the evidence-grade declaration, twice in a row.

**v1.4.1 disposition.** The two conjuncts are replaced by exact certificates: `φ⁻⁸ = (47 − 21√5)/2` with `47² > 21²·5 > 45²` (hence `0 < φ⁻⁸ < 1` exactly) for `C34`, and the symbolic identity `B(c_crit) = ρ` together with the exact rational comparisons `m_max < T₂ < 2m_max/(1+m_max²)` (hence `−1 < c_crit < 0`) for `C61`; the decimal `c_crit = −0.63692416…` remains as a *display* value in the claim and detail strings, which are not part of any PASS decision. More importantly, **the property is no longer asserted in prose: it is row `G69`**, which re-parses this script, isolates every `C`-row block and the `ok` argument of its `add()` call, and fails if any line feeding `ok` contains `sp.N`/`float(`/`mp.mpf(`/`np.`/`abs(`/`1e-`/`evalf`. Two consecutive audits found a false declaration of exactly this property; a guard with a kill condition and a live-fire case (n′) is the proportionate answer (kernel §10-5), and it is the only guard added in this version. Count of findings: `C32` (v1.3 audit), `C34` and `C61` (v1.4 audit) — all three repaired; `G69` reports `none` on the 69 rows of v1.4.1.

**Recorded, not adopted.** (i) The v1.4 auditor offers a conservative alternative to F-2 — retype `C34`/`C61` as `V` and publish `C=42, V=6, universal 38`. This version takes the other option the auditor names (exact replacement), so the census keeps `C=45, V=4` with the three added rows; the alternative census is recorded here so that a reader can reconstruct it. (ii) The auditor's boundary sub-review supplies an optional uniform two-step contraction argument for the carrier iterate under a **non-convergent** unital refresh sequence (Lemma 5.10 assumes a convergent reference). It is a clarification of an existing conclusion, not a found defect and not a requested theorem; it is recorded here and left for a future version rather than inserted without independent proof. (iii) The auditor's `T = T_w` example for the *maximally mixed* reference is degenerate; the full-rank example above is used instead.

**Delivery.** `HISTORY_APPEND_M68_H0300_H0301.md` was listed in the v1.4 manifest but absent from the upload the auditor received (§2 of the report). **Label: MANUSCRIPT DRAFT.** The v1.4 audit returned **AUDIT-PASS-MINOR (S1)** with no S2 and no new counterexample to the central results, and its disposition is that after these local repairs **a SUPPORT-scope freeze can be considered** and research effort should move to §6.7 step 0 and the action-derived coupling `G` rather than to further versions of this paper. The next audit is therefore a **delta/targeted (narrow)** check of exactly four things — the class hypothesis in Cor 7.1 and in the `C20`/`C61` claim strings, the exactness of the `C34`/`C61` certificates and the new guard `G69`, the `T`/`T_w` quantifier, and the comparison-row/step-number/version synchronisation (AUDIT §14). Promotion to REVIEW READY follows that check; promotion to PREPRINT READY additionally requires L6 (H1–H15, with the seed packet attached), the full-text novelty sweeps, a third-party reproduction of the v1.4.1 ledger, and the R-row embedding of the live-fire log. **This version does not declare a freeze**: the v1.4 audit explicitly did not approve one, and its verdict applies to the v1.4 hashes, not to the files repaired here.
